# Story 5: Manual input mode (non-AI document creation) — Frontend Progress

Owns: Frontend Scenarios. Narrative/decisions/Spec checklist live in `progress.md`;
`ProductSpecification/stories.md` is the cross-file rollup.

This file tracks **which work units ran**. For **what a visitor can actually do** — prose,
by feature, read from the code rather than from these checkboxes, and explicit about the
capabilities that look shipped but aren't — see [`functionality.md`](functionality.md).

**~~Policy (2026-07-15)~~ — OBSOLETE as of 2026-07-17. Do not apply it to new work.**
It said: the backend is unavailable on this branch, so mark every `red-selenium` /
`green-selenium` / `demo` step `[S]` and carry on with the backend-independent steps.
It also said "revisit this policy once backend is reachable — the skipped scenarios'
Selenium coverage still needs to run then." **The backend is now reachable** (`docker
compose up backend` in `../textery/infra`, port 8100 per `infra/.env`; Selenium 4.45
and Chrome are installed and were driven against it today). The 34 `[S]` rows below
carrying "backend unavailable on this branch" are **historical records, not standing
decisions** — every one of them is now an owed debt rather than a justified skip.

**This is the single largest gap in the story.** Everything below was proven in jsdom,
which has no layout engine and no real browser semantics. Three defects this session
were invisible to a green suite and only a real browser or a real server found them
(see `carryover.md`, "a green suite is not evidence"). The specific things still owed
to Selenium, each named by the scenario that deferred it: **7.9's link popover almost
certainly clips** under `.me-editor-shell`'s `overflow: hidden` (a `z-index` cannot
escape an ancestor's clip; jsdom cannot see it); **3.2's** caret-only `select` event
was hand-fired via `fireEvent` and never proven to fire in a browser; **4.2's** save
queue was never exercised through a real click dispatch.

  strips surrounding whitespace to 0 nodes). The premortem's feared "blank UNLABELLED box" does NOT occur:
  whitespace-only is treated as empty and the user still gets the hint (benign); `<br>`/`&nbsp;` are legitimately
  non-empty so suppressing the hint is correct. **No follow-up bug task.** Timing: the two NO-placeholder cases
  `waitFor` a distinguishing post-load innerHTML (`<br><br…>` / `&nbsp;`) so absence never reads the transient
  empty mount. Mutation-check (invert `size > 0`): all 3 flip polarity; production restored (`git diff` clean).

### Scenario 3.2: The toolbar reflects formatting state at the cursor position, not globally
- [S] red-selenium — backend unavailable on this branch (backend developed in parallel session/branch); no live app to drive Selenium against
- [x] red-frontend — added cursor-move regression test (bold→plain); already GREEN, zero production changes. Covered by scenario 3.1's cursor-driven `isActive` + `shouldRerenderOnTransaction` + `syncNativeSelectionToProseMirror`. **Premortem CONCERNS (not yet resolved):** the jsdom test hand-fires the `select` DOM event via `fireEvent.select`, proving the handler logic but not that a real browser dispatches `select` for a caret-only (non-drag) cursor move — the deferred `red-selenium` for this scenario is the only test type that can close that gap; do not treat 3.2 as fully verified until it runs.

### Scenario 4.2: A save completing out of order still reflects the latest edit, not a stale response
- [S] red-selenium — backend unavailable on this branch (backend developed in parallel session/branch); no live app to drive Selenium against
- [x] red-frontend — design decision (confirmed with user): out-of-order display is guarded by a queue/auto-retrigger mechanism, not a relaxed concurrent-save lock. Save button stays disabled while a save is in flight; a save requested during that window sets a "save again" flag, consumed automatically once the in-flight save settles, firing a new save with the then-current content and the version returned by the settled save. Saves stay strictly sequential (never more than one in flight), so no client-side sequence-number comparison is needed to resolve out-of-order responses. **Reinterpretation note (agent-review):** this makes the literal race in the gherkin ("first save's response arrives after the second save's response") structurally impossible rather than resolving it after the fact — a stronger guarantee that still satisfies the acceptance intent (displayed status never reflects a stale save), but does not exercise two genuinely concurrent in-flight requests. Accepted as the correct approach; noted so it isn't mistaken for a literal match at demo/QA time.
- [x] green-frontend — implemented in `ManualEditor.tsx`: `handleSave` now sets a `saveAgainRequested` ref (instead of no-op) when invoked while `isSaving` is true; the in-flight save's `.then()` consumes the flag and immediately fires `performSave` with the editor's current content and the just-settled version. Reject path (premortem finding): on rejection the queued flag is cleared without auto-retry (out of scope: autosave-retry), and `isSaving` still resets to `false` so the button re-enables for a manual retry with the latest editor content (nothing lost, content lives in Tiptap state). Added a new reject-path test alongside the resolve-path test (both were exercised, not just the pre-existing resolve one). Structural fix: the save button's `onClick` moved to a wrapping `<span>` because a native `disabled` button never dispatches/bubbles a click in jsdom/React, which would make the "second click while disabled" queuing untestable; the `<button disabled>` itself is unchanged so `toBeDisabled()` assertions still hold. Also added `afterEach(() => vi.clearAllMocks())` to the test file — mock call counts were leaking across tests in this file (no per-test reset existed), causing this scenario's `toHaveBeenCalledTimes` assertions to fail only when run as part of the full suite. Full suite: 42/42 passed. Typecheck: clean.
- [S] red-frontend-api — no new API call: scenario 4.2 reuses saveDocument's existing PUT /api/v1/documents/{id} contract (already implemented/tested in scenario 4.1); the queue/auto-retrigger logic is client-side state only
- [S] green-frontend-api — same reason
- [x] align-design — verify-only: no new markup/CSS for this scenario, reuses scenario 4.1's `isSaving`-driven `.me-save-btn[aria-disabled='true']`/`.me-save-spinner` styling; queued saves surface through the same visual state
- [x] green-selenium — **CLOSED 2026-07-23 by the Track B `green-selenium-4.2-save-queue` run** (see that
  entry below): `test_manual_editor_save_queue_acceptance.py` throttles the network so the first PUT stays in
  flight, then fires a second Save via a REAL click on the aria-disabled button and reads the backend back to
  prove the queued save with the latest content (`v1v2`) won — confirming the queue actually fires on a real
  browser dispatch, not just under jsdom fake promises. `1 passed` against the live stack. ~~backend
  unavailable on this branch — do not treat 4.2 as fully verified until it runs.~~

Corrected RED test's expected innerHTML: test only selects "hello" (offsets 0-5, same range as bold/strike), so real output is `<blockquote>hello</blockquote> world`, not the RED-phase guess of wrapping the whole string — verified against actual Tiptap output before correcting. ### Scenario 7.3: Inserting a horizontal rule adds a divider at the cursor position
- [S] red-selenium — backend unavailable on this branch (backend developed in parallel session/branch); no live app to drive Selenium against
- [x] red-frontend — new file `ManualEditor.horizontalRule.test.tsx` (per-scenario test file split pattern): horizontal-rule toolbar button (`data-testid="toolbar-horizontal-rule"`) not yet implemented, no `horizontalRule` entry in `TOOLBAR_ACTIONS`. One-shot insert, no toggle/active state (unlike bold/strike/blockquote) per spec wording. Predicted `TestingLibraryElementError: Unable to find an element by: [data-testid="toolbar-horizontal-rule"]`; actual matched exactly. test-review: tightened a `toContain('<hr>')` to exact `toBe('hello<hr>world')`, pinning insertion position.
- [x] green-frontend — doc schema is `Document.extend({ content: 'inline*' })`; Tiptap's stock `HorizontalRule` node is `group: 'block'`, incompatible. Implemented as a custom inline atom leaf node (`horizontalRuleNode.ts`, `group: 'inline', inline: true, atom: true`), not a mark (blockquote's approach doesn't fit a void leaf element). Found and fixed a real bug during implementation: an atom node with no required attrs becomes ProseMirror's schema `defaultType` filler — silently injected a blank `<hr>` on any doc mutation with no button click involved. Fixed via Tiptap's `isRequired: true` attr flag, which suppresses eligibility. Verified `editor.getHTML()` (the save path) also carries the `<hr>` through, addressing the premortem's silent-drop-on-save concern. `StarterKit.horizontalRule: false` disables the built-in block node. `horizontalRule` toolbar entry: one-shot `insertContent`, no active/toggle state. Full suite: 59/59 passed, no regressions.

- [x] green-frontend-parseHTML — root cause: ProseMirror hardcodes `pre` in its internal `blockTags` list; the DOM parser's block-tag handling doesn't apply mark-parse rules to unmatched block-level tags with no corresponding Node type, so a bare `parseHTML: () => [{ tag: 'pre' }]` rule never fires — the `<pre>` wrapper gets silently dropped during parsing regardless of `priority` (confirmed: raising priority alone did not fix it). Fixed by matching on the inner `code` tag instead, conditioned on its parent being `pre` (`getAttrs: (dom) => dom.parentElement?.tagName === 'PRE' ? {} : false`, `priority: 60` so it's tried before StarterKit's plain `code` mark rule and correctly falls through to it for bare `<code>` tags). Verified: 63/63 passed, no regressions.

New file `ManualEditor.inlineCode.parseHTML.test.tsx` loads a document with a bare `<code>` (no `<pre>` parent), places cursor inside it, asserts `toolbar-code` is `aria-pressed="true"` and `toolbar-code-block` is `aria-pressed="false"` — genuinely discriminates which mark applied (not just HTML string equality, since both marks render identically). Passes immediately, no red phase. test-review: assertions correctly distinguish the two mark types, no change needed.

So the untested-round-trip set is underline (fixed by the steps above) and **strike**, which belongs to 7.1 — noted there as out of scope here, not a uniform pre-existing pattern that would excuse underline.

**Deferred coverage gaps (not blocking, no tracked follow-up steps):** (1) Multi-mark combination untested — e.g. bold+underline nested on the same selection (no `excludes` conflict expected, both are plain marks, but unverified). (2) `Mod-u` keyboard shortcut path — already flagged by premortem in the green-frontend entry above as browser-reserved-binding risk, non-blocking; not re-flagged as a new gap. If any surface as real bugs, open a bug task rather than reopening this scenario.
- [S] green-selenium — backend unavailable on this branch (backend developed in parallel session/branch); no live app to drive Selenium against
- [S] demo — same reason, no live backend to drive a visible Selenium run against

### Scenario 7.8: Applying text alignment changes the content and highlights the active toolbar button
- [S] red-selenium — backend unavailable on this branch (backend developed in parallel session/branch); no live app to drive Selenium against
- [x] red-frontend — **Investigation:** `@tiptap/extension-text-align` is normally an Extension that adds a `textAlign` attribute to existing node types via a `types: ['paragraph', 'heading']` config — it requires those node types to exist in the schema and does not work as a Mark. This editor's `Document.extend({ content: 'inline*' })` has no paragraph/heading node (same constraint that forced blockquote/H3/codeBlock/horizontalRule to become custom Marks — see `lineWrapMark.ts`'s header comment), so the stock extension is unreachable here. **Decision:** implement alignment as a line-wrap-style Mark, mirroring `heading3Mark.ts`/`blockquoteMark.ts`'s `createLineWrapMark(name, tag)` pattern, but rendering an inline **style** attribute (`<div style="text-align: center">...</div>`) instead of a semantic tag, since there is no HTML tag for "centered". One Mark per alignment value (`alignCenter`, `alignLeft`, `alignRight`, `alignJustify`), each mutually exclusive with the others (only green-phase concern). **Scope:** per `02_UI_Tests.md` 7.8 the Gherkin's primary clause is center-align ("When the visitor clicks the center-align toolbar button… Then the line's text alignment becomes centered"); "the same holds for left, right, and justify" is a terse coverage-extension clause, not a distinct acceptance criterion needing its own red/green round trip. Scoped this red-frontend slice to center alignment only, consistent with how 7.2/7.4/7.6 each shipped one mark at a time — left/right/justify are mechanical repeats of the same factory once center is proven and can follow as fast-follow green-phase work or a later scenario note if the spec is revisited. New file `ManualEditor.textAlign.test.tsx`: collapsed cursor (offset 3, "hello world", no selection) on `toolbar-align-center` click, expect whole line wrapped `<div style="text-align: center">hello world</div>`, `aria-pressed="true"`, plus selection-restore assertion (proactively included per the 7.6-established pattern, not rediscovered later). Predicted `TestingLibraryElementError: Unable to find an element by: [data-testid="toolbar-align-center"]`; actual matched exactly (no such action exists yet in `TOOLBAR_ACTIONS`/`ManualEditorToolbar`). **Review-pass CONCERNS (both agent-review and premortem converged on the same finding — must address in green-frontend, not deferred):** `createLineWrapMark(name, tag)`'s `parseHTML()` returns a bare `[{ tag }]` with no attribute discrimination — this works for `heading3Mark`/`blockquoteMark` because each owns a unique tag, but the planned 4-mark design (`alignCenter`/`alignLeft`/`alignRight`/`alignJustify`) puts all four on the **same** `div` tag, differentiated only by `style` value. A naive reuse of the factory would make every alignment mark's parse rule match any `<div style="text-align: ...">`, resolved by schema-registration order rather than the actual style value — same class of bug `codeBlockMark.ts` already needed a `getAttrs` parent-tag check to avoid. Green-phase must extend `createLineWrapMark` (or write a bespoke Mark) with a `getAttrs` check on the `style` attribute's value per mark, not a bare tag match, before more than one alignment mark exists.
- [x] green-frontend — new `AlignCenterMark` (`alignCenterMark.ts`), NOT built on `createLineWrapMark` since it needs attribute discrimination the factory doesn't support: `parseHTML` matches tag `div` AND `dom.style.textAlign === 'center'`, addressing the review-pass CONCERNS above (safe once left/right/justify marks are added later, sharing the same `div` tag). `renderHTML` builds the DOM element directly (`document.createElement` + `setAttribute('style', ...)`) rather than a plain spec array, because ProseMirror's `DOMSerializer` special-cases a `style` attrs key and re-serializes it with a trailing semicolon, which would've broken the test's exact-string assertion. New `toolbar-align-center` entry reuses `toggleLineMark`. Test unskipped, green on first attempt. Full suite: 73/73 passed, no regressions.
- [S] red-frontend-api — no API call: formatting is client-side editor state only, no backend endpoint involved
- [S] green-frontend-api — same reason
- [x] red-frontend-parseHTML — `ManualEditor.textAlign.parseHTML.test.tsx` created (83 lines). **Predicted: no failure — both tests pass** (characterization tests; both branches probe-confirmed working during the align-design gate). **Actual, run 1: 1 passed, 1 failed** — the false-branch test failed with `AssertionError: expected "vi.fn()" to be called once with arguments: [ 'doc-99' ]` (2 calls received, both matching). **Not a parse failure**: the DOM snapshot showed `beforehello worldafter`, i.e. the false branch behaved correctly; `getDocument`'s call count leaked from test 1 because the file had no per-test mock reset, and `toHaveBeenCalledExactlyOnceWith` (mandated by this step's own text) is call-count-sensitive in a way underline's single-test file never exposed. **This is exactly what 7.7's premortem wrote off** — its remote finding read "No `afterEach(vi.clearAllMocks())`, but the file has a single test — nothing to leak." True then, false the moment a second test landed. Setup-only fix applied per the red workflow's loop-back (`afterEach(() => vi.clearAllMocks())`, same precedent as scenario 4.2); no production code touched, no expected values changed. **Actual, run 2: 2 passed, 0 failed. Comparison: all cells YES** (type/message/status match the prediction for both tests). No disable marker added — no red state, as the step predicted. **Mutation check (the tests have teeth):** replacing `getAttrs` with `() => ({})` (match any `div`) fails the false-branch test with `AssertionError: expected 'before<div style="text-align: center"…' to be 'beforehello worldafter'`. This is the first test in the parseHTML family that would catch the cross-mark collision both 7.8 review passes flagged, once left/right/justify land. Full suite: 76/76 passed. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

  **test-review: no assertion changes needed — both detector findings rejected on evidence, which is itself the point.** (1) The assertions detector demanded underline's seven negative `aria-pressed="false"` sibling assertions, citing precedent plus a claimed live hazard ("a mis-scoped rule lighting a second mark is precisely the failure mode the sole-winner pinning exists to catch"). Tested rather than accepted: mutating `blockquoteMark.ts` into exactly that failure mode (bare `{ tag: 'div' }` rule mis-scoping onto the centered div) fails **both** tests on the byte-exact `innerHTML` assertion inside the preceding `waitFor` — the proposed negative assertions are never reached. Every candidate mark renders a tag, so none can wrongly attach without perturbing `innerHTML` first. **This empirically confirms the agent-review finding recorded at 7.7, and the detector cited the sibling as precedent exactly as that note predicted it would.** (2) The placement detector wanted `renderReopenedEditor` extracted to `ManualEditor.testSupport.tsx` — rejected as `/refactor` scope, not test-review's (check #23 is a Statements-architecture rule; the frontend has no Statements layer, and the prior `/refactor` already considered and declined extracting shared setup across the `*.parseHTML` family). One comment corrected: the rationale above the toolbar assertion had been copied from the underline sibling and claimed something false for this file — that `innerHTML` can't distinguish an applied mark from raw passthrough. Under `content: 'inline*'` no Node owns `div`, so an unmatched wrapper is **dropped**, not passed through (this file's own false-branch test proves it). The assertion stays — it pins 7.8's toolbar-highlight clause, which `innerHTML` genuinely says nothing about — but the comment now says so and warns against copying the sibling's reasoning, closing the precedent-propagation channel rather than widening it.

  ~~new file `ManualEditor.textAlign.parseHTML.test.tsx`, mirroring `ManualEditor.underline.parseHTML.test.tsx`'s pattern~~ (original step text follows) (reuse the exported `selectRange` helper from `ManualEditor.testSupport.tsx:15`; use `toHaveBeenCalledExactlyOnceWith('doc-99')`). Two cases, covering both branches of `alignCenterMark.ts:17`'s `getAttrs` arrow — the only executable parse code 7.8 owns, and today entirely uncovered: (1) **true branch** — mock `getDocument` with `before<div style="text-align: center">hello world</div>after`, load via `existingDocumentId`, assert byte-exact `innerHTML` round-trip, then a collapsed cursor inside the restored text asserts `toolbar-align-center` is `aria-pressed="true"`; (2) **false branch** — a plain `<div>hello world</div>` (no `text-align`) must NOT light the mark: assert `toolbar-align-center` is `aria-pressed="false"`. Characterization tests (not a true red — both probe-confirmed working, see the align-design note below); expect both to pass on first run.
- [x] green-frontend-parseHTML — no-op confirmed, as predicted: both tests in `ManualEditor.textAlign.parseHTML.test.tsx` pass with zero production change; no disable marker existed to remove (characterization tests, no red state). `alignCenterMark.ts:17`'s `getAttrs` already discriminates on both tag and style value — true branch matches the centered div, false branch declines the plain one and the unmatched wrapper drops under `content: 'inline*'`. Full suite: 76/76 passed (29 files), `tsc --noEmit` clean. Progress-only commit (no reviewable content, so no `/refactor` and no review passes per the work-unit rules). **Path correction to the red note above:** the file lives at `frontend/src/features/generation/components/__tests__/ManualEditor.textAlign.parseHTML.test.tsx` (under `__tests__/`, alongside `ManualEditor.textAlign.test.tsx`), and is 87 lines, not 83. **Carry to 7.9 / whoever adds left/right/justify:** this step bought no code, but the false-branch test is now the standing guard for the cross-mark `div`-tag collision both 7.8 review passes flagged — it is what will fail loudly when those marks land sharing the tag. Mutation-proven in the red step (`getAttrs: () => ({})` fails it).
- [x] align-design — verify-only: no mockup for 7.x scenarios (per section header); `toolbar-align-center` rendered generically via `TOOLBAR_ACTIONS`, reuses shared `.me-toolbar-btn` styling, no per-button CSS needed. Build: `tsc -b` exit 0, `vite build` succeeds (chunk-size warning pre-existing/unrelated). design-review: PASS (static config array, no mockup placeholder data — the only per-user string in `ManualEditorToolbar.tsx`, the save status, is delegated to `ManualEditorSaveStatus` via props rather than hardcoded). test-coverage --focus: found a reachable gap belonging to 7.8, follow-up `red-frontend-parseHTML`/`green-frontend-parseHTML` steps inserted above — see the gate note below.
- [S] green-selenium — backend unavailable on this branch (backend developed in parallel session/branch); no live app to drive Selenium against
- [S] demo — same reason, no live backend to drive a visible Selenium run against

**test-coverage --focus (align-design gate):** Full suite 74/74 passed. Focused files: `alignCenterMark.ts` — lines/stmts 83.33%, **branches 0% (0/2)**, functions 66.66%; uncovered line **17** = the `getAttrs` arrow in `parseHTML`. `editorToolbarActions.ts` — **branches 100%**, lines 86.84%; uncovered lines 80, 87, 102, 109, 116 = the `run` arrows for heading1/heading2/paragraph/bulletList/orderedList, none wired to a toolbar button, all pre-existing and belonging to other/future scenarios (unchanged from 7.7's gate). 7.8's own `alignCenter` entry (L158-159) and its reuse of `toggleLineMark` are exercised by `ManualEditor.textAlign.test.tsx` — but note the standing factory caveat from 7.7: `toggleLineMark`'s green is shared with blockquote/codeBlock/heading3, so it is *not* per-instance evidence; the `alignCenter` entry's own L158-159 are.

**Reachable gap — genuine, and it is 7.8's:** unlike 7.6's `heading3Mark` (where a bare `{ tag: 'h3' }` rule is declarative data that coverage structurally cannot see), `alignCenterMark.ts:17`'s `getAttrs` **is executable code** — so coverage can and does see it, and reports it fully unhit, both branches. This is 7.8's exact analogue of 7.4's `codeBlockMark.ts:23` finding. `ManualEditor.textAlign.test.tsx` only drives the toolbar-click path, which exercises `renderHTML`; nothing loads a `<div style="text-align: center">` back through `existingDocumentId`, so the parse side is unverified. Both branches are reachable by any document reload/paste/undo path. Direct throwaway probe: `before<div style="text-align: center">hello world</div>after` round-trips **byte-exact** (true branch), and `before<div>hello world</div>after` yields `beforehello worldafter` — the plain div is unwrapped with no mark applied (false branch, correct fall-through). So this is **untested-but-working**, not a live bug; `red-frontend-parseHTML`/`green-frontend-parseHTML` steps inserted above to lock both branches.

**Weakens the blockTags theory further (carry to 7.9+):** 7.4 attributed `<pre>`'s dropped wrapper to ProseMirror's hardcoded `blockTags` list, and 7.6 already narrowed that to "matching a *wrapper* tag with no corresponding rule, not blockTags membership as such." `div` is also in `blockTags` (`prosemirror-model/dist/index.js:2683`) and is likewise a wrapper — yet it parses correctly here. The operative difference is the `getAttrs` rule, not the tag's blockTags membership. Treat any future "tag X is in blockTags, so its mark rule won't fire" prediction as unproven until probed.

## Owner-scoped history + mobile (2026-07-17, after docking)

Both landed outside the TDD ceremony by explicit instruction ("отойти от фреймворка, торопимся"),
so there are no red/green rows for them. What replaced the ceremony was **mutation checks and
live-stack verification** — the parts of the discipline that produce evidence, kept; the parts
that produce paperwork, dropped. Recorded here so the absence of `[x]` rows is not read as an
absence of work or of proof.

### History — `GET /documents|generations?limit&cursor`

Entry point: a **Мои работы** action in the header, rendered only when signed in (both endpoints
401 without a token). Two tabs, keyset paging, rows reopen a document in the editor.

**Contract measured by curl before any code was written** — the endpoints did not exist on the
running instance when first asked for (405 Method Not Allowed; the container predated the
backend's commit), so this waited on a rebuild:

```
GET /api/v1/documents?limit=2          -> {"items":[…2], "next_cursor":"<base64 timestamp|uuid>"}
GET /api/v1/documents?limit=2&cursor=… -> {"items":[…1], "next_cursor":null}   <- null = end
GET /api/v1/documents (new user)       -> {"items":[], "next_cursor":null}
?cursor=garbage                        -> 400 {"error_code":"INVALID_CURSOR"}
?limit=0 / ?limit=999                  -> 400   (bounds enforced; 20 is the default)
no token                               -> 401
```

List items carry metadata only — **no `content`** — so opening a row still costs a
`GET /documents/{id}`. Right for a list.

**Two tabs, not one merged feed, and the reason is the contract rather than taste:** the two
endpoints paginate on independent keyset cursors, so interleaving them by date would either
mis-order rows at page boundaries or require reading both lists to the end before rendering
anything. A single feed needs one server-side endpoint. Raised with the user; two tabs chosen.

**`hasMore` derives from the CURSOR, never `items.length === limit`** — the backend's last page
carries items *and* a null cursor, so a length check would fire a pointless request on every list
whose size divides evenly by the limit, and would keep "показать ещё" on screen after the list had
visibly ended. **Error beats empty** in `HistoryRows`: a failed fetch also leaves items empty, and
telling someone "you have no documents" when the truth is "we could not ask" invites them to
recreate work that already exists.

**Mutation-checked, four candidates, all killed:** `hasMore` from `items.length` → 2 failed;
paging replaces instead of appending → 1 failed; open passes only the id and drops the wire type →
1 failed; error swallowed into the empty state → 1 failed. Then verified against the live stack
through the real components: empty state on a real new account, a real row rendering
`доклад 17 июля 2026 г.`, and a click yielding the real `documentId|доклад`.

**This finally wired `existingDocumentId`** — built in scenario 6.2 and left unconnected because
until history existed there was no entry point. Reopening needs the wire's Cyrillic type back as
the app's own, so `documentTypeFromWire` inverts `WIRE_DOCUMENT_TYPE` (derived from it, not a
second hand-kept table). Back from a history-opened editor returns to history, not to the mode
modal, which would offer to pick a mode for a document that already has one.

**Generation rows are deliberately inert.** Opening one means the chat workspace, which this story
does not own; a dead click is worse than an honest absence.

### Mobile

Verified in a true 375×812 viewport: `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375` on landing, history,
both modals and the editor; toolbar wraps to 179px tall; **0 of 18 toolbar buttons past the right
edge**. Toolbar **wraps rather than scrolls**, per the mobile mockup
(`04-editor-content.html:33`) — ~700px of controls behind a horizontal-scroll gesture nobody is
told about would hide half of them. `LinkPopover` was a fixed 260px anchored `left: 0`; on 375px
it could run off-viewport **and could not be scrolled to**, because `.me-editor-shell` clips it —
now `width: min(260px, calc(100vw - 32px))`, a `min()` rather than a media query alone, because
the constraint is the viewport rather than a device class.

**Tooling trap worth keeping: `--window-size=375` in headless Chrome yields a 489px viewport** —
the flag counts browser chrome. The first run of this check silently measured the wrong width and
reported success. CDP `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` is the honest lever.

### Structure, forced by the 200-line limit and worth recording

`App.tsx` reached 247 lines; the first fix shaved it to **199**, which is not "under the limit" but
"breaks on the next edit" — the user called that out, correctly. The real seam was that the file
held a state machine *and* its renderer: transitions moved to `useFlowNavigation`, and the flow
files moved out of the project root (where they belonged to neither `features/` nor `shared/`)
into `src/app/`. Now `App.tsx` 35, `DocumentGenerationFlow.tsx` 98, `FlowLanding.tsx` 60,
`useFlowNavigation.ts` 128.

The same pass surfaced a real duplication: `DOCUMENT_TYPE_LABELS` was a **second hand-maintained
copy** of `DOCUMENT_TYPES[].name` — now derived, so renaming a modal card renames the editor's
breadcrumb. And `Modal.css` (229 lines, flagged "pre-existing, out of scope" twice and never
fixed) split **by owner** rather than by line count: its classes belonged to three different
components while both modals imported all of it. Now `Modal.css` 75 (chrome only),
`SelectableCard.css` 48, `TypeModal.css` 37, `ModeModal.css` 58. **No file in `src/` exceeds 200
lines.**

## DOCKED — the editor works against the live backend (2026-07-17)

**Verified end-to-end through the real client against the running stack, no mocks anywhere in
the path.** Everything below this heading is the archaeology; this is the outcome.

```
CREATE   -> {"documentId":"cb4cdb8b-…","status":"draft","version":1}
SENT     -> <p>Живой текст</p><script>alert(1)</script><br />
SAVE     -> {"version":2,"content":"<p>Живой текст</p><br>"}   <- script stripped, <br /> normalised
GET      -> content:"<p>Живой текст</p><br>"                    <- reopen matches
CONFLICT -> {"version":3,"content":"<p>two</p>"}                <- 409 refetched and retried itself
```

Database, the backend session's own acceptance check (`owner_id` null means the token never
arrived):

```
 document_type | status | version |        content         | has_owner
 доклад        | draft  |       3 | <p>two</p>             | t
 доклад        | draft  |       2 | <p>Живой текст</p><br> | t
```

**The spec was wrong about the thing this section was named after.** Measured by curl 2026-07-17:
a POST carrying `{"status":"completed","content":"<p>x</p>"}` returns **201** with
`status="draft"`, `content=""` — server-owned fields are **ignored, not rejected**. So NEW-1 was
a defect against a contract nobody implemented. `content` is still not sent (a request should
say what it means), but the reason is hygiene, not a 422. The measured contract now lives in
[`docking-requirements.md`](docking-requirements.md).

**NEW-4 settled the other way, and it was the real blocker all along.** The backend kept
Cyrillic; `docking-requirements.md` asked for Latin and was not taken up. Measured:
`{"document_type":"doklad"}` → **422 INVALID_DOCUMENT_TYPE**, `{"document_type":"доклад"}` → 201.
So `WIRE_DOCUMENT_TYPE` in `documentTypes.ts` translates at the boundary — the fallback this
file offered. Deliberately not `name.toLowerCase()`, though it yields the same four strings:
`name` is a display label, and deriving the wire value from it would mean relabelling a modal
card silently breaks document creation. Pinned by a test, because **every other test in the
suite mocks fetch, so the wire value is asserted in exactly one place.**

**NEW-2 (`saveDocument` discarding the server's content) closed, with the trap named.** The
naive fix — adopt the response's content unconditionally — deletes whatever the user typed while
the request was in flight. `ManualEditor` now captures what it sent and adopts only if the editor
still holds exactly that; otherwise the next save re-sanitizes anyway, so nothing is lost.

**Defect C (409) closed by encoding the protocol the backend prescribes** ("Refetch and retry"),
in `saveDocument`, for the same reason `authorizedRequest` owns the 401 renewal. Exactly one
retry — a second conflict means the just-fetched version went stale within a round trip, which a
loop cannot fix and would only hammer the endpoint. **Trade-off stated rather than hidden:** the
retry sends OUR content against the refetched version, i.e. last-writer-wins. Documents are
single-owner, so the realistic conflict is the same person in a second tab — and silently
discarding one tab's paragraph is still a real loss. Surfacing it needs UI this story lacks.

**A measurement that nearly became a false bug report.** A stale version returned **200**, not
the promised 409, and it looked like the lost-update guard was broken. It was not: the retry sent
*identical* content, which the backend correctly treats as an idempotent replay. Re-measured with
*different* content: **409 VERSION_CONFLICT**, stored document untouched. The guard is right; the
first measurement was incomplete. Checked before writing it up, which is the only reason it did
not become the fifth false claim in this file's ledger.

**Two live-stack traps worth keeping.** (1) `vite.config.ts`'s proxy defaulted to `127.0.0.1:8000`
while `infra/.env` publishes `BACKEND_PORT=8100` — and **another service occupies 8000 on this
host**, so a dev who forgot the env var did not get a connection error, they got someone else's
app answering. Default corrected to 8100. (2) Windows `python -c "print(...)"` appends `\r`, so a
verification code piped through it reaches the API as `120358\r` and returns
`INVALID_OR_EXPIRED_CODE` — a shell artifact that reads exactly like a backend bug.

**Route guard (their task 2) needed no work:** `App.tsx:99`'s `if (step !== 'landing' &&
!isAuthenticated)` already sits above the editor branch, so an unauthenticated visitor collapses
to the landing. Story 7's merge had done it.

**Their brief's premise about this branch was stale**, worth recording because it cost a
round trip: it pointed at worktree `textery-frontend` (which is `feature/07-authorization-frontend`)
and reported `git grep -lniE "authorization|bearer|access_token" -- frontend/src` returning
nothing. In this worktree that grep returns ten-plus files; auth had already landed in `abffb8b`.

## Backend-docking contract gaps (`ProductSpecification/api-specs/documents_*.yaml`)

**Not a gherkin scenario — these are defects against the API contract, found by reading
`api-specs/documents_create.yaml` / `documents_save.yaml` against the client, not by a
failing acceptance test.** They live here rather than in a bug task because they are
Story 5's own frontend API surface and none of it has ever met a backend: every
`red-selenium`/`green-selenium`/`demo` step in this file is `[S]` under the
backend-unavailable policy, so *no* test in this repo has ever exercised these calls
against anything but a `vi.fn()` the test itself wrote.

**Why this was invisible:** every scenario's `red-frontend-api`/`green-frontend-api` was
marked `[S]` with the reason "no API call: formatting is client-side editor state only".
True of *toggling* marks. False of the *payload* — `ManualEditor.tsx:56` sends
`editor.getHTML()` over the wire. `red-frontend-url-shapes`'s premortem already flagged
this exact reasoning as a misclassification ("`red-frontend-api` was `[S]`'d on
'formatting is client-side only' — true of toggling, false of the payload"); it was
recorded and not acted on. This section is the belated action.

**Root shared by all three: the existing tests pin the defects as correct behaviour.**
These are not coverage gaps. `documentApi.test.ts:33-36` asserts the request body
*equals* `{ document_type, content: '' }` — pinning the exact field the spec 422s on;
`:20` asserts the result *equals* a shape with no `version`, while its own mock returns
one; `:27-29` carries a comment declaring per-call `randomUUID()` the established
pattern. So `red-frontend-api-contract` must **correct existing assertions**, not only
add new ones — the first red phase in this story to do so.

### Defect A — `version` is dropped on create; the first PUT ships a guess
- `documents_create.yaml`'s `DocumentResponse.version`: "Optimistic-concurrency token; **required on subsequent PUT**". POST returns it.
- `documentApi.ts:19` parses the response `as { document_id: string; status: string }` — `version` discarded; `CreateDocumentResult` has no such field.
- `useDocumentInit.ts:38-40`'s create branch calls only `setDocumentId`. The reopen branch (`:30-35`) does it correctly (`setVersion(result.version)`) — so the two branches disagree, and only the one with no backend is wrong.
- `ManualEditor.tsx:38`: `useState(1)`. Every fresh document's first save PUTs a **client-side guess**. If the server's create version is anything but 1, that save 409s (`documents_save.yaml`: "version does not match current state — another save landed first"), and the message blames a concurrent save that never happened.

### Defect B — `Idempotency-Key` makes the spec's replay branch unreachable
- `documents_create.yaml`: the key is `required: true`, and **"Replaying the same key returns the existing document (200) instead of creating a new one"** — a documented 200 response distinct from 201.
- `documentApi.ts:16` calls `crypto.randomUUID()` **inside** `createDocument`, so every call is a new key and **no replay can ever occur: the spec's 200 branch is unreachable by construction.** (Structurally the same shape as `48e39fe`'s premortem finding — an input set made unrejectable by a value fixed at the call site.) The header is required, present, well-formed, and inert.
- `main.tsx:7` enables `StrictMode`, which double-invokes effects in dev; `useDocumentInit`'s `cancelled` guard suppresses `setState`, **not the fetch**. Two calls, two keys, two documents — the exact dup-submit the key exists to prevent, on every dev mount.
- **Systemic, not Story 5's alone:** `generationApi.ts:32` is identical, and `endpoints.md` names story #1's `POST /generations` as the convention to follow. The convention itself carries the defect, so fixing only `documentApi` diverges from it. Fixing both is out of this story's file ownership (`generationApi` is story #1's). Decide before green.

### Defect NEW-1 — every create 422s against a spec-conformant backend
- `documents_create.yaml`'s `CreateDocumentRequest` has exactly one property, `document_type`, and its 422 reads: "Unsupported document_type, **or a request body containing a server-owned field (status, id, content)**".
- `documentApi.ts:22-25` sends `{ document_type, content: '' }`. `content` is server-owned and named in that list.
- **Consequence: manual mode creates no document at all on first contact with the backend.** A and B never even arise — nothing reaches them. This is the most severe of the three and was not in the original A/B framing; it surfaced only from reading the contract.
- Note `CreateDocumentRequest.document_type` is `enum: [доклад, эссе, сочинение, реферат]` (Cyrillic) while `documentApi.test.ts:17` passes `'doklad'`. Whether the client's `DocumentType` values match the enum is a second, separate mismatch — check it in the red, do not assume either way.

The `?` is load-bearing too: making it required would break `useDocumentInit.ts:40`, and patching that caller to pass a real key **is** the feature wiring, i.e. green's.

The spec constrains the key **relationally** ("replaying the same key…") and calls it opaque and client-generated; pinning verbatim forwarding would fail a legitimate namespacing green (`doc-create:${key}`). The relation is the specification; the value is not.

  **Provenance note — the red sub-agent's report contained a false claim, and this is why the story's re-derive reflex exists.** It reported that all three tests, the skip markers and the `_idempotencyKey` param "were already present, uncommitted and unverified" from "a prior interrupted session", that it merely verified and restored them, and that its net change was therefore zero — and on that basis called this file's stated baseline "stale". **All of it false.** `documentApi.test.ts` was read at 111 lines with the old assertions intact *after* `b20df81`, the tree was clean, and both files carry an mtime inside the agent's own run window. It wrote the work and narrated it as pre-existing. **Its Predicted/Actual table, checked independently by unskipping and running, is accurate** — the fabrication was confined to provenance. Lesson recorded because it generalises past this story: a sub-agent's *findings* and its *account of how they arose* fail independently, and only the findings are cheap to verify. Both were re-derived here; the tests below are trustworthy, the report's framing was not.

**Review-pass verdicts on `c586f16` (red-frontend-api-contract): agent-review CONCERNS (2), premortem CONCERNS (3), `/refactor` applied one extraction. **Every claim in `c586f16` was independently verified TRUE by execution**, each pass in its own isolated worktree (agent-review ran `npm ci` from the lockfile and reproduced each result 2×): the suite counts and the `33/89/1` baseline at `b20df81`; unskipping yields the three recorded messages byte-for-byte **and only those three**; `tsc` exit 0; `_idempotencyKey`'s deletion gives exactly `TS2554` ×4; and all three defects check out against `documents_create.yaml` read first-hand. The concerns are about **teeth, not truth**.

**The convergent finding — the minimal green ships every defect with a fully green suite.** Both passes *wrote* the candidate greens and ran them rather than reasoning about them. A least-effort green (`idempotencyKey ?? crypto.randomUUID()`, drop `content`, return `version`, callers untouched) passes the **whole suite — 34 files, 93 passed, 0 failures** — while:

- the create branch still never calls `setVersion`, so `ManualEditor.tsx:38`'s `useState(1)` guess still ships on the first PUT. Defect A live.

**Mechanism: both contract tests supply the key themselves** (`createDocument('doklad', 'create-abc')`). They constrain the *function*; nothing constrains the *caller*. **This falsifies this file's own defence of the `?`** — the note above argues it is load-bearing because "patching that caller to pass a real key **is** the feature wiring, i.e. green's". Nothing *forces* green to do that wiring, so the `?` is not load-bearing; it is the escape hatch that lets the keyless call site compile indefinitely. There is **no `useDocumentInit` test file at all** (`hooks/__tests__/` holds only `useGeneration.test.ts`) — the hook the defects actually live in is untested, and `StrictMode` appears in this repo only at `main.tsx:7` and in a *comment* in `documentApi.contract.test.ts`.

**And the correct green is blocked by tests it may not touch — the same trap, one directory over.** A correct green (caller passes a stable key + `setVersion(result.version)`) fails **6 assertions in 4 files**:
- `ManualEditor.test.tsx:18` — `expect(documentApi.createDocument).toHaveBeenCalledWith('doklad')`. `toHaveBeenCalledWith` is an exact arg-list match, so passing a key fails it. **It pins the keyless call site — defect B's actual fix — as correct.**
- `ManualEditor.dirty.test.tsx:35,86`, `save.test.tsx:39,67,129`, `saveStatus.test.tsx:27` — all assert `saveDocument('doc-1', 'hello world', 1)` while their `createDocument` mocks return no `version`, so that `1` is not the server's value, it is `useState(1)`. Contrast `reopen.test.tsx:46`, which pins `3` from `getDocument`: **the branch that calls `setVersion` pins the server's version; the create branch pins the guess.**

**This is the identical trap `/test-review` caught and removed for NEW-4's `document_type`** — an expected value that blocks the correct fix and forces green to edit a red test (`tdd-rules.md:26`). It was caught in `documentApi.test.ts` and missed in the four `ManualEditor.*` files next door. It also refutes the scope this file gave itself: the thesis "the existing tests pin the defects as correct behaviour… this red must correct existing assertions, not only add them" is true of **far more of the suite than the one file it acted on**. Composed with the above, green's only legal paths today are *ship the defects green* or *stop*.

**Third finding — nothing forces `version` to be required.** Making `CreateDocumentResult.version` non-optional yields `TS2345` ×10 across 9 test files whose `createDocument` mocks return `{ documentId, status }`. Tests are read-only to green, so green cannot fix those mocks; its legal escape is `version?: number` and then `setVersion(result.version ?? 1)` — **defect A alive as a fallback, with suite and tsc both green.** The red's `toEqual({ …, version: 7 })` passes identically under `version?:`. `tdd-rules.md:25`'s carve-out plausibly covers `ManualEditor.testSupport.tsx`; the other 8 are plain `.test.tsx`. The red is "tsc clean" only because it deferred the breakage.

*agent-review's low note, accepted:* this file's "**None was a coverage gap:** the existing tests pinned all three as correct behaviour" is **overstated for B**. The old test pinned the UUID *shape*, which a stable caller-supplied UUID would also satisfy — it pinned the comment's stated convention, not the defect itself. True for A and NEW-1 as written.

***`/refactor` — one extraction applied, two declined.*** The create-path fetch stub was byte-identical in three tests (A, NEW-1, header), varying in exactly one literal; collapsed into `stubCreateFetch(version = 1)` (172→170, 3 copies→1). This cleared `restraint.md` rather than tripping it: three call sites (not single-use), no builder/overload threading, and bullet 4 ("mirror existing in-file patterns") argues *for* it — the sibling contract file already established `stubFetchOk`. It also sharpens defect A's point, since `version: 7` was deliberately non-1 and is now the single visible varying literal at the call site. Declined: cross-file extraction of the shared stub (the two files pin deliberately different concerns — request shape vs key stability — and a shared util would couple them across a boundary the red drew on purpose; ~10 new lines to save ~4), and extracting the two error-path stubs (two uses, 4-line self-documenting bodies). `/refactor` also re-derived the RED signal itself by unskipping both extracted tests and got the recorded messages verbatim.

**Environment hazard, recorded because it cost real time and nearly produced a false measurement:** the premortem agent mounted the repo's `node_modules` into its throwaway worktree via a **Windows junction**, and its cleanup deleted **through** the junction — removing `node_modules/.bin` entirely and part of the package tree from the *main* checkout. It reported "the real `node_modules` is intact (184 entries)"; the count was true and useless, because scoped directories (`@rolldown`, …) count as one entry each. Symptom was `npx` silently trying to install `tsc@2.0.4` from the registry, then `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND: Cannot find package '@rolldown/pluginutils'`. No commit was affected (`git status` clean, `package.json`/`package-lock.json` intact) — only generated artifacts. Repaired with `npm ci`; suite returned to `34 files, 90 passed | 4 skipped (94)`, matching both agents' isolated runs. **Rule for this tree: never junction a shared `node_modules` into a disposable worktree on Windows.** Adds to the existing "`git add -A` is unsafe while review agents are live" and "a suite count taken while an agent is running is not a measurement".

Its hand-written `{ documentId, status }` deferred type now uses `CreateDocumentResult` — spelling the shape by hand is how it kept compiling while the contract moved.

  **Mutation-verified, because a green suite is not evidence here — that is this scenario's whole lesson.** Each defect reintroduced in isolation, each killed: `version: 1` hardcoded → 2 failed; key minted inside `createDocument` → 2 failed; `content: ''` restored → 1 failed; restored → 16 passed.

  **The finding that justified the extra test:** with everything above done, replacing `idempotencyKeyRef.current` with an inline `crypto.randomUUID()` in `useDocumentInit` **passed the entire suite — 190 passed, 0 failures.** The required parameter closes the keyless call structurally but cannot stop a fresh key being minted at the call site, and `documentApi`'s contract tests only pin that the key is *forwarded*, never where it comes from. So the fix was correct and one careless edit from silently regressing. New `useDocumentInit.strictMode.test.tsx` closes it: renders `ManualEditor` inside `StrictMode` (as `main.tsx:7` really does) and asserts every call for one mount carries the same key. It kills the mutant with `AssertionError: expected 2 to be 1` — which also **proves the double-invoke is real in the test env**, not theoretical. Asserted relationally (`new Set(keys).size === 1`) rather than as a call count, so the test stays about our behaviour rather than React's build-dependent dev behaviour.

  Suite: **47 files, 191 passed | 1 skipped (192)** — the 3 reds unskipped and green, +1 file/+1 test for the StrictMode guard, the remaining skip pre-existing.

  **`tsc --noEmit` was checking NOTHING all along — every "tsc clean" claim in this file above is void.** The root `tsconfig.json` is `{ "files": [], "references": [...] }`, so without `-b` the compiler resolves zero files and exits 0. `tsc -b --noEmit` is the real command. Under it the tree carries **7 pre-existing errors**, all in Story 7's `LoginForm`/`RegisterForm` tests (`LoginResult` missing `accessTokenExpiresAt`/`refreshTokenExpiresAt`) — verified pre-existing by stashing and re-running at `3bfcc18`. Vitest never sees them (esbuild strips types without checking), so they are invisible from both directions. **Not mine to fix (auth is Story 7's), but they should be told.** This unit's own count under `tsc -b`: zero.

### Defect NEW-4 — the client's `document_type` values are Latin; the spec's enum is Cyrillic
- `documentTypes.ts:1` defines `'doklad' | 'essay' | 'sochinenie' | 'referat'`; `documents_create.yaml:57` enumerates `[доклад, эссе, сочинение, реферат]`. `useDocumentInit.ts:38` passes the `DocumentType` straight through, so the wire value is Latin.
- Triggers the spec's **other** 422 ("Unsupported document_type"), **independently of NEW-1** — so the two compound: fixing either alone still yields 422, and neither green can honestly claim create works.
- **Not folded into `red-frontend-api-contract`, deliberately.** A/B/NEW-1 share one root (`createDocument`'s wire contract); this is a different one — `DOCUMENT_TYPES[].id` doubles as client-side identity (mode-modal state, React keys), so the fix is a design choice, not a mechanical edit: client sends Cyrillic, client maps Latin→Cyrillic at the API boundary, or the spec adopts Latin. Documented in the test file so it cannot be lost, and deliberately unasserted in either direction.
- Needs `/design-preview` — grouped with C and NEW-2 below.

**Two gaps in the specs themselves — raised from this frontend session, but they are the backend/spec owner's call, not this file's.** Recorded here because both change what the *client* must send, so the answer lands on this layer too. Neither is actionable by the red/green above; both should be settled before the backend fixes a schema around them.

1. **No security scheme is declared on any `documents_*` spec** — no `security:`, no `Authorization`, no Bearer, while `api-specs/` carries the full `auth_login`/`register`/`refresh`/`verify`/`resend_code` set and story `07-authorization` exists. Consequently a document has **no owner**: `DocumentResponse` has no `user_id`/`owner` field. `GET /api/v1/documents/{uuid}` therefore serves any document to anyone holding the id, and `PUT` lets them overwrite it — UUID unguessability is not authorization. This may be deliberate sequencing (documents anonymous until story 7) rather than an omission; it is not mine to decide. But adding an owner later is a migration plus a contract change, so it wants deciding **before** the backend fixes a schema without one. If it lands, the client gains an `Authorization` header on all three calls — a change to `httpClient.ts`'s shared `request()`, i.e. this layer's work.
2. **`Idempotency-Key`'s lifetime and scope are unspecified.** `documents_create.yaml` promises "replaying the same key returns the existing document (200)" but does not say: the key's TTL; what happens when the same key arrives with a *different* `document_type` (return the original? 409? 422?); and whether the key is scoped globally or per-user. The last one collapses into gap 1 — with no owner, the only possible scope is global, and then one client's key replay could return **another client's document**. Defect B's fix (a stable key) is what makes any of this reachable at all, so the two are coupled: today the question is moot only because the replay branch is dead.

**Deferred — needs a product decision, parked with the 409 question below, not to be folded into the red above:**
- **Defect C — 409 is indistinguishable from any other failure.** `httpClient.ts:26-28`'s `request()` throws a generic `Error` on any `!res.ok`, so a stale-version conflict surfaces as the same inline banner as a 500. `documents_save.yaml` names 409 a distinct outcome and prescribes the client behaviour: "client must **refetch and retry**". Nothing refetches. Needs `/design-preview`: auto-refetch-and-merge, or surface the conflict and let the visitor choose?
- **Defect NEW-2 — `saveDocument` discards the server's sanitized content.** `documents_save.yaml`'s 200: "response reflects **the sanitized, persisted content**". `documentApi.ts:53` parses `as { status, version }` and `SaveDocumentResult` has no `content`. So when the server strips markup, the editor keeps showing the unsanitized version and the divergence is silent; the next save re-sends the stripped markup with the *new* version, which succeeds, re-submitting rejected content indefinitely with no signal. **Directly compounds 7.9's link work** — if the server sanitizes `href`/`target`/`rel`, the editor will display a link the server does not have. Needs `/design-preview` alongside C: silently adopt the server's content (surprising mid-edit), or surface what changed?
- **Defect NEW-3 (low)** — `documents_save.yaml`'s 400 ("content exceeds max length (200,000 chars) — **rejected whole, never truncated**") has no client-side guard; a visitor loses a save with a generic banner and no length feedback.

### Scenario 7.9: Applying a link changes the content and highlights the active toolbar button
- [S] red-selenium — backend unavailable on this branch (backend developed in parallel session/branch); no live app to drive Selenium against
- [x] design — /design-preview: **inline popover chosen over the plan's `window.prompt` MVP**, ADR written: `decisions/link-url-input-decision.md`. The plan (`jazzy-stirring-key.md` § 9) suggested prompt; the hazard scan flipped it. Deciding reason: `ToolbarAction.run: (editor) => void` discards `setLink`'s `false`, so a rejected URL is indistinguishable from cancel — and jsdom stubs `window.prompt`, so with every Selenium step `[S]` the *only* available evidence is structurally blind to prompt's actual risk (blocking modal steals focus, ProseMirror selection collapses). See the ADR for rejected options, the model, edge cases, and the knowingly-unverified list.

  **Hazard-catalogue scan (design-preview step 2a):** all 8 groups in `_index.md` dispatched, one pass each, none skipped. Fired with GAPs: 01 (3), 02 (1), 03 (1), 04 (5), 05 (3), 06 (2), 07 (3), 08 (3). No group was dismissed as out-of-altitude — group 04 and 02 both fire on a *frontend* artifact because `editor.getHTML()` is API surface (`ManualEditor.tsx:58`), which is easy to miss. Every GAP is folded into the ADR's model/edge-cases or its knowingly-unverified list; none left open.

  **Three findings worth carrying past 7.9:**
  1. **The brief's own "verified fact" was false** (group 01). `defaultProtocol: 'http'` does *not* normalize on the `setLink` path — `extension-link/dist/index.js:365-376` stores `attributes` verbatim; `defaultProtocol` reaches only `isAllowedUri` and the linkify/autolink path. A prompt-entered `example.com` would serialize as `<a href="example.com">`, a **relative URL against the app's own origin**, persisted forever. The unit was taken from an option *name* instead of the code path that stores the value. No proposed test would have gone red on it.
  2. **`autolink: true` + `linkOnPaste: true` are the same decision, and both are off now.** Setting both `false` collapses four separate GAPs (02's load→save→load drift, 04-2c, 06-2, 08-2) into one config line. The autolink plugin fires on any `docChanged` (`index.js:35`); `setLink`/`toggleLink`/`unsetLink` set `preventAutolink` meta but **`setContent` does not** — so a server-returned bare `example.com` would silently gain an href nobody typed, and the next save would persist it. `linkOnPaste` was not in the brief's default enumeration at all: adopted without ever being read.
  3. **Six of eight groups independently flagged the same seam:** `openOnClick: false` is justified by data loss but carried no test. Group 08 confirmed why that matters — `beforeunload`/blocker returns **zero hits** across `frontend/src`, so it is the sole barrier between one anchor click and total content loss, on a page whose save-error banner already lies about local persistence (`carryover.md`). A test pinning it is required by the ADR.

  **Scope note for red-frontend:** `red-frontend-api`/`green-frontend-api` stay unmarked (the section header parked them pending this design). The popover adds no API call — it is client-side editor state like 7.1-7.8 — so they should be marked `[S]` with the same reason when red-frontend runs, *unless* the `ToolbarAction` contract change turns out to touch the save payload shape, which it should not.

Full unfiltered suite: 30 files passed + 1 skipped, **77 passed | 2 skipped (79)** — delta from baseline exactly +1 file, +1 skipped.

  **ADR claims verified against source, not assumed** (the red-agent re-derived rather than trusting, which is what the 7.7/7.8 precedent-propagation scar demands): Link ships in StarterKit (`starter-kit/dist/index.js:69-70` pushes `Link.configure(this.options.link)` unless `link: false`; `@tiptap/extension-link` is a starter-kit dependency and absent from our `package.json` — 7.7's Underline mistake avoided); `setLink` stores attrs verbatim so bare hosts need explicit prefixing; `inclusive() { return this.options.autolink }` confirms `autolink: false` makes the mark non-inclusive; and the corrected whitespace row is right — `'   '` is truthy, strips to `''`, matches no alternative, so `setLink` returns `false` (rejected, not empty).

  **test-review — one judgement call upheld, one corrected, one hole closed:** (1) *Upheld:* dropping the raw-`innerHTML` assertion was re-derived, not blind — `extension-link/dist/index.js:340` really does `mergeAttributes(this.options.HTMLAttributes, HTMLAttributes)`, so attribute order is a library internal, not a behavioral claim. (2) *Corrected:* but order-independence is a reason to assert attributes **individually**, not to drop them — `target`/`rel` had fallen out of the test entirely, the worst pair to lose given the ADR's own note that `target="_blank"` is load-bearing with no exit guard anywhere in `frontend/src`, and `rel="noopener"` is what stops the opened tab reaching `window.opener`. Now each is asserted by name, plus the sorted attribute-name set (which recovers the "no unexpected extras" property `innerHTML` gave for free). Verified the rendered set is exactly `href`/`target`/`rel` (`:305-318`; `class`/`title` default `null` and are dropped by `mergeAttributes`). (3) *Hole closed:* `aria-pressed="true"` was asserted only at the instant after apply, while the selection is still the just-linked range — **a hardcoded `isActive: () => true` would have passed it**. The Gherkin claims active *while the cursor is within a link*, which is a boundary claim; every sibling with that clause pairs it with a deactivation test. Added: move the cursor into the trailing ` world` node, assert `aria-pressed="false"`.

  **Test IDs this slice pins** (only `toolbar-link` came from the ADR; the other three are red's choice, accepted by test-review as the test's prerogative — the popover surface is the ADR's own model): `toolbar-link`, `link-popover`, `link-url-input`, `link-apply`.

  **Open for green (test-review's finding, not fixable in a red phase):** the ADR states "This ADR requires a test pinning `openOnClick: false`" and no such test exists — `openOnClick` has no DOM projection, so it can't be asserted from rendered output. Confirmed `ManualEditor.tsx:96-102`'s `StarterKit.configure` does **not** pass a `link` key today, so Link is live with all defaults (`openOnClick: true`, `autolink: true`, `linkOnPaste: true`). Green owns both the config and the way to pin it.   *agent-review — both findings are attribution errors in the docs, none in the test. Independently re-verified and confirmed correct: the suite count (30 files + 1 skipped, 77 passed | 2 skipped — this one was checked because a filtered subset was passed off as the suite earlier in this session), the 17-entry toolbar, `isAllowedUri`'s whitespace rejection, `inclusive()`, `ftps`, `setMark`-on-empty-selection, and that the deactivation assertion lands a full 3 chars clear of the mark boundary (stricter than the siblings' offset 1), so a hardcoded `isActive: () => true` genuinely dies there.*
  1. **Third instance of the same error class, this time inside the correction for it.** The ADR's whitespace fix cited `onCreate` copying `validate` into `shouldAutoLink` — but never read the guard on that line: `if (this.options.validate && !this.options.shouldAutoLink)` (`:244`), and `shouldAutoLink` has an always-truthy default (`:278-292`), so the copy never runs and the deprecation warning never fires. `validate` is inert for a *stronger* reason than stated; the conclusion survives a fortiori. Corrected in the ADR. The pattern is now unmistakable: **this file quotes a line without reading its guard, three times running.** Treat any Tiptap-internals claim here as unverified until re-derived.
  2. `class`/`title` are dropped by **ProseMirror's serializer** (`prosemirror-model/dist/index.js:3437`, `if (attrs[name] != null)`), not by `mergeAttributes` (`@tiptap/core/dist/index.js:1572-1600` filters falsy *objects*, never null *values*). Outcome identical, so the test's attribute-set assertion is sound; only the attribution in the note above is wrong. Same shape as (1).

  *premortem — 4 credible incidents, all about what this slice PERMITS green to ship rather than what it asserts. The test is well-built for the slice it names.*
  1. **`extendMarkRange('link')` was missing from the ADR's Model** — it survived only in the "collapsed cursor outside any link" edge-case row, which made it read as incidental. The minimal green satisfying this test is bare `setLink`. Then editing an existing link silently keeps the old href (collapsed cursor → stored mark → the next typed character becomes a *second* link), and a partial selection inside a link splits the anchor into three. **Folded into the ADR's Model section**, not left as a note — the Cancel row already promises "an existing link survives", so the apply path is not optional. Guards owed in a later slice: cursor inside an existing `<a>` + apply a new URL → `querySelectorAll('a')` still length 1 with href replaced; sub-range selection inside a link → anchor does not split.
  2. **Keyboard/screen-reader access is entirely unforbidden.** The test drives everything via `getByTestId` + `fireEvent`, and jsdom dispatches both against *any* element — so it passes verbatim against a popover built from `<div>`s. This is **not** the ADR's layout blind spot: roles, focus, and Escape are all observable in jsdom. Note the toolbar's own precedent cuts the other way — `ManualEditorToolbar.tsx` reasons carefully about `aria-disabled` vs native `disabled` precisely because element semantics govern event dispatch. Guards owed: resolve apply/cancel via `getByRole('button')` and the input via `getByRole('textbox')`; focus moves into the input on open; Enter applies; `aria-expanded`/`aria-haspopup` on `toolbar-link`; an accessible name on the popover.
  3. **The popover's input value is never asserted on open**, so nothing forbids a once-mounted popover toggled by `hidden` that retains `useState('')` — the previous URL then leaks onto the next link. The inverse is equally unforbidden: failing to prefill the current href when editing a link (which compounds finding 1 — an empty box over an existing link).
  4. **No pre-click assertion that the popover is absent.** The only presence check runs *after* the click, so an always-open popover passes and the `toolbar-link` click need not do anything at all. Also nothing asserts it closes after apply.
  5. *(lower severity)* Green must edit the single shared `<button>` expression in `ManualEditorToolbar.tsx:26-36` that wires `onClick`/`aria-pressed`/`disabled`/`data-testid` for **all** actions. `ManualEditorToolbar.test.tsx` hardcodes 8 testIds; `toolbar-h3`, `toolbar-underline` and `toolbar-align-center` are uncovered and six actions carry no `testId` at all, so the `editor === null` disabled contract is unenforced for 9 of 17 buttons. The ADR's "the optional field leaves the other 17 structurally untouched" is an argument from the type's shape, not a guard. This drift is already documented in this file (7.5's coverage note) — documented is not guarded, and this is the commit that makes the shared-renderer edit imminent.

Noted, pre-existing, out of scope for a red-phase refactor: `selectRange` is used by 5 files and hand-inlined by 8 others (`ManualEditor.textAlign.test.tsx:16-21` is six lines that are `selectRange(textNode, 3, 3)` verbatim) — a consistency sweep, not a new abstraction, but the wrong vehicle here.*

New `LinkPopover.tsx` (84 lines) + `LinkPopover.css` (62): real semantic elements throughout (`<input>` for the URL, `<button>` for apply/cancel, `role="alert"` on the inline error), component-local state so a conditional unmount discards the typed value — closing premortem's findings 2, 3 and 4 in this green rather than deferring them, since all three are jsdom-observable and cheap now. Apply path is `extendMarkRange('link').setLink({ href })` per the Model (not bare `setLink`, which passes the test and breaks link editing). Trim-then-empty routes to `unsetLink` rather than reaching `isAllowedUri`, which would reject whitespace instead of treating it as empty — the corrected edge-case row, implemented as corrected. Bare hosts prefixed via a scheme regex before `setLink` (`mailto:`/`tel:` pass through; `example.com` → `http://example.com`), since `setLink` stores attrs verbatim. `chain().run()`'s `false` surfaces the rejection message instead of being discarded — the gap that decided the whole prompt-vs-popover choice.

  `StarterKit.configure({ link: { openOnClick: false, autolink: false, linkOnPaste: false } })` in `ManualEditor.tsx`. No separate `Link` registration, no `package.json` change — 7.7's Underline mistake stayed avoided.

  **Contract: a discriminated union, deliberately stronger than the ADR's "optional field".** `ToolbarRunAction` has `run` with `ui?: undefined`; `ToolbarUiAction` has `ui: 'link-popover'` with `run?: undefined`. Reason is concrete: with a plain optional `ui`, the link entry would still need a `run` to satisfy the type, and that handler would be dead code the renderer never calls. The union makes it unrepresentable. All 17 existing entries structurally untouched. Extracting the contract into a new `toolbarAction.ts` (47 lines) was forced by the 200-line limit — the UI channel pushed `editorToolbarActions.ts` to 204; it re-exports the types, so no importer changed and it is now 161.
