Full unfiltered suite: **31 files, 78 passed | 1 skipped (79)** — delta from the red baseline is exactly the one test moving skipped→passing, no other file changed state. (The remaining skip is Task 5's intended red, not this story's.) `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. All files under the cap: `LinkPopover.tsx` 84, `LinkPopover.css` 62, `ManualEditorToolbar.tsx` 81, `editorToolbarActions.ts` 161, `toolbarAction.ts` 47, `ManualEditor.tsx` 191.

  **The popover is probably broken in a real browser, and nothing here can see it.** `.me-editor-shell` carries `overflow: hidden` (`ManualEditor.css:74`) and is an ancestor of the toolbar; the popover is `position: absolute` with `z-index: 20` in an anchored wrapper — the right shape, but a `z-index` cannot escape an ancestor's `overflow: hidden`. It will clip. jsdom reports green because it has no layout engine. This is exactly the blind spot the ADR admitted when the popover was chosen over `window.prompt` ("the decision relocated the blind spot rather than removing it"), now concrete rather than theoretical: **only `green-selenium` can confirm it, and that step is `[S]` on this branch.** Do not read 7.9's green suite as evidence the control is usable.
**Review-pass verdicts on `d6eb173` (green-frontend): agent-review CONCERNS, premortem CONCERNS, `/refactor` applied 2 changes. Non-gating — the commit stands — but unlike the recent passes, these found real defects in shipped code, so follow-up red/green steps are inserted below (7.2's cursor-fix precedent).** Verified and holding: `extendMarkRange` is real and does fix href-replace-in-place; disallowed protocols do reject with a visible message (traced `javascript:alert(1)` and `htp://x` through `isAllowedUri`'s regex); the `overflow: hidden` clipping flag is accurate (`.me-editor-shell` at `ManualEditor.css:74` is the toolbar's direct parent via `ManualEditor.tsx:169`); cancel/empty/whitespace-trim/bare-host/non-inclusive all match the ADR table.

**Real defects found (each gets a step below):**
1. **`example.com:8080`, `localhost:3000`, `my.host:9000` are rejected as "неверный адрес"** — `HAS_SCHEME = /^[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9+.-]*:/` includes `.` in the class, so a dotted *host* is indistinguishable from an RFC-3986 *scheme*. Verified by execution. The regex is correct per RFC and wrong per user intent, and this is the one path where the error message actively misleads — the address is genuinely fine. Not in the ADR's table.
2. **Open the popover on an existing link and press Apply → the href is destroyed.** The field is always `useState('')` (`LinkPopover.tsx:24`) and empty routes to `unsetLink` (`:35-40`). Each ADR row is individually satisfied; their **composition** is destructive and is not in the ADR. The user cannot see or amend the current href, and open→Apply is the most likely gesture on a link. The whole `extendMarkRange` rationale exists to make editing work — but the popover never surfaces what is being edited.
3. **Selection drift while the popover is open bypasses the error signal.** No `useEffect`, no `selectionUpdate` subscription, no click-outside — so the user can click into the document, return, and Apply against a now-collapsed cursor. `setMark` on an empty selection **writes a stored mark and returns `true`**, so `if (!applied)` never fires: the popover closes silently and the *next typed character* becomes the link. This bypasses the one signal the whole prompt-vs-popover decision was made to buy. Distinct from the ADR's "collapsed cursor outside any link" row, which pins a *deliberate* collapsed cursor. A second, self-inflicted variant: a **rejected** apply still dispatches — `CommandManager.createChain` (`@tiptap/core/dist/index.js:83-86`) dispatches `tr` before returning the boolean, so `focus()`/`extendMarkRange` have already moved the selection by the time `setLink` returns false; the user's retry runs against a selection the failed attempt moved.
4. **`user@example.com` → `http://user@example.com`** — silently linked as an http URL with userinfo. Passes `isAllowedUri`, no error, wrong result. Plausible input; not in the ADR's table.
5. **Enter and Escape do nothing** (zero `onKeyDown`/`onSubmit`/autofocus hits in `LinkPopover.tsx`). Enter in a single-field URL box is the universal gesture — and one `window.prompt` gave for free. The ADR explicitly left Escape/click-outside "to `red-frontend` to decide deliberately"; red never pinned it and this green **resolved it by silence**. Needs a decision, not just a test.
6. **`aria-pressed` and `aria-expanded` on the same control** (`ManualEditorToolbar.tsx:53,55`) — a toggle button and a disclosure at once, which is conflicting ARIA. `aria-pressed` reports where the *cursor* is, not the popover's state, and `.me-toolbar-btn[aria-pressed='true']` is the only state rule in the CSS: the button highlights while the popover is **closed** (cursor in a link) and shows nothing while it is **open**. `aria-expanded` has no assertion at all.
7. *(low)* All 17 pre-existing buttons gained a bare `<span>` wrapper (`ManualEditorToolbar.tsx:44`), which becomes the flex item in the button's place. No current selector is child/sibling-based and flex blockifies the span, so it likely renders identically — but "structurally untouched" is true of the *type* only, and jsdom cannot say otherwise. Rendering the wrapper only for `action.ui` costs nothing and makes the claim true.

***`/refactor` applied:*** extracted `ManualEditorBreadcrumb.tsx` (37) + `.css` (58), taking `ManualEditor.tsx` 191→172 and `ManualEditor.css` 113→54 (all six `.me-breadcrumb*`/`.me-chip-icon` classes were exclusive to the moved block); and added `--btn-primary-bg`/`--btn-primary-fg` tokens to `index.css`, replacing literal `#f5f5f7`/`#111214` in `LinkPopover.css`, `ManualEditorToolbar.css` and `ChatWorkspaceDoc.css` — this green added the **third** copy of that pair, and restraint does not shield production duplication. Suite byte-identical after (31 files, 78 passed | 1 skipped), tsc clean. **`useDocumentSave` declined a third time**, and the reasoning is worth keeping: the line pressure was real but the save machine is the wrong relief valve — `performSave` and `useEditor`'s config are *mutually* entangled (`performSave` needs `editor.getHTML()`; `handleDOMEvents.input` needs the hook's `isSavingRef`/`saveAgainRequested`), so a hook must be declared before `useEditor` and could only reach the editor through lazy getters closing over a TDZ `const editor` — indirection without clarity. The breadcrumb bought 19 lines at zero coupling instead. `normalizeHref` stays in `LinkPopover.tsx`: it reads domain-ish, but its *reason* is Tiptap-adapter knowledge (`setLink` stores attrs verbatim, never reaching `defaultProtocol`), and that comment only makes sense beside the `setLink` call. **Flagged, pre-existing, out of scope:** `Modal.css` is **229 lines** — a live violation of the 200-line hard limit, untouched by this commit.

- [x] red-frontend-url-shapes — new file `ManualEditor.link.urlShapes.test.tsx` (136 lines after test-review): 5 `it.skip` red tests + 2 live regression guards. **Product decision taken with the user:** `user@example.com` → `mailto:`, not a rejection and not today's silent `http://`. **Predicted/Actual/Comparison — all cells YES on the first run, no prediction loop:** tests 1-2 (`example.com:8080/path`, `localhost:3000`) predicted `AssertionError: expected to have a length of 1 but got +0` at `expect(anchors).toHaveLength(1)` — `HAS_SCHEME` matches the dotted host, the URL passes through unnormalized, `isAllowedUri` rejects, `setLink` returns false, no anchor renders; actual identical. Test 3 (`user@example.com`) predicted the failure lands one line *later*, on the href assert rather than the length assert (`expected 'http://user@example.com' to be 'mailto:user@example.com'`), because `http://user@example.com` passes `isAllowedUri` so the anchor does render; actual identical, including the failing-assert position.

  **The red-agent re-derived the mechanism from `isAllowedUri`'s regex source rather than trusting the premortem's execution result** — the right reflex given this file's standing scar (three wrong Tiptap-internals claims in a row). Both it and test-review independently traced why alternative 3 (`[a-z0-9+.\-]+(?:[^a-z+.\-:]|$)`) can never consume a dotted host: every backtrack position is followed by a letter, `.`, or `:`, all excluded from the trailing class, and it never reaches end-of-string.

  **Two deliberate scoping choices, both upheld by test-review:** `localhost:3000` is a *separate* test from `example.com:8080`, not a variant — the obvious shortcut fix ("a scheme only if the prefix has no `.`") passes the first and still breaks the second. And `queryByRole('alert')` is asserted absent on every test, because defect 1's harm is not the missing link, it is that the visitor is told a *fine* address is bad; without it a green that silently no-ops would pass. (Honest note: on tests 1-3 the alert assertion is currently unreachable — `toHaveLength(1)` fails first — so it only starts carrying weight once green produces the anchor. Right ordering, not a defect.)

  **test-review's finding — the red-agent was one rung short of its own standard.** Its justification for splitting `localhost` out applies with equal force one level down, and it stopped there: `localhost` is a well-known name and therefore *the* most likely string for green to special-case or reach for via a hostname allowlist — a green that does so passes the suite and breaks every intranet host. Added `myserver:8080` (arbitrary dotless host, kills the allowlist green) and `admin@sub.example.co.uk` (second, structurally different email — local part *and* host differ — so the mailto rule must generalize). Also added the missing `textContent === 'hello'` pin to both live guards, which the red tests had and the guards didn't. Verified the `mailto:a@b.com` guard is genuinely load-bearing, not decorative: a naive green (`url.includes('@') && !url.includes('://')`) yields `mailto:mailto:a@b.com`, which **passes** `isAllowedUri` via the `mailto:` alternative, so the anchor renders and only the `toBe` catches it. Declined to duplicate the exhaustive `rel`/`target`/`getAttributeNames` pin here — `ManualEditor.link.test.tsx:40-51` owns it, and repeating it across seven href-focused tests is duplication, not teeth. **Stated honestly rather than overclaimed:** a green can still hardcode all five strings; no finite fixture set forecloses that. What the suite achieves is making hardcoding cost five unrelated branches across three shape families with no shared structure — strictly more work than fixing the discriminator. That is the achievable bar.

  Full unfiltered suite: **32 files, 80 passed | 6 skipped (86)** — live passes unchanged from the 78+2 baseline, delta is exactly the 5 red skips. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

  **Note for green** (from the red-agent): the `mailto:` disposition means `normalizeHref` needs three outcomes, not two (pass through / `http://` / `mailto:`), and the email branch must be decided **before** the scheme check — `user@example.com` has no `:` at all, so it reaches the default-prefix path regardless of how `HAS_SCHEME` is tightened.
**Review-pass verdicts on `520a621` (red-frontend-url-shapes): agent-review CONCERNS, premortem CONCERNS, `/refactor` extracted an assertion helper.** Everything the commit claimed was independently re-verified and holds: the three host-port rejections and the silent `http://user@example.com`, both re-derived by *executing* `isAllowedUri`'s regex rather than reading it; the failing-assert positions (tests 1-3 die on `toHaveLength(1)`, tests 4-5 one line later on the href); the suite count; and that the `mailto:a@b.com` guard is genuinely load-bearing (`mailto:mailto:a@b.com` does pass `isAllowedUri` and render).

**Both passes converged independently on the same gap, each proving it by writing a candidate green that passes all 7 fixtures:**
- **`@` in a *path* is not an email, and nothing pins that.** `youtube.com/@vsauce` → `mailto:youtube.com/@vsauce`, which passes `isAllowedUri` via the `mailto:` alternative — anchor renders, no alert, saved. `vk.com/@durov`, Medium and Mastodon handles, and `?email=a@b.com` query strings all share the branch. **This is the file's own thesis turned on the file:** it argues at length that the discriminator must be structural rather than a name — splitting `localhost` from `example.com:8080`, then `myserver` from `localhost` — and then stops at the *first rung* of the email family. `admin@sub.example.co.uk` varies the local part and the host but is the same shape. A second sub-shape shares the failure: a green excluding via `!url.startsWith('mailto:')` rather than "has a scheme" also passes all 7 and mangles `https://youtube.com/@vsauce`.
- **The fixtures are 100% ASCII in a Russian-language editor.** The natural structural fix — a positive host-shape check — is an ASCII regex, which drops `кремль.рф` / `культура.рф` / `правительство.рф` into whatever fallback green writes; `isAllowedUri`'s `[^a-z]` alternative then lets the Cyrillic first char through, so the anchor **renders** as a relative URL against our own origin. That is precisely the harm `LinkPopover.tsx:7-11` says the prefix exists to prevent, now aimed at every `.рф` citation in every доклад. The deeper version: the 7 fixtures each match a *branch*; **nothing pins the fallback** for input matching none (`/docs` → `http:///docs` or a relative `/docs`, same hole).

*agent-review's two low notes, both accepted:* (1) the commit message's `role="alert"` rationale ("without it a silently no-op green would pass") is **false as written** — a no-op green renders no anchor and dies on `toHaveLength(1)` first, which the progress note above already concedes; the tests are right, the commit message overstated what earns their keep and contradicted the note it summarized. (2) **The ADR does not record the `mailto:` decision** — its edge-case table is the normalization contract and lists two outcomes; the product decision makes it three, and there is no email row. Under-described, not contradicted. Fixed below.

***`/refactor` — extracted `expectSoleLink(contentArea, href)`, declined the `it.each`, and reframed the question I handed it.*** The premise was off: setup was *already* extracted into `applyLinkUrl`, so the seven bodies were duplicate **assertions**, not setup — and `restraint.md`'s carve-out shields setup while saying in terms it does **not** apply to assertion-logic duplication, so the AGAINST case never reached this. But 7.7's `it.each` precedent didn't transfer either: there the rows were interchangeable instances of one rule, whereas rows 1→2→3 here are a deliberate *ladder* (each exists to defeat a specific shortcut green) and row 4 is a different root cause; folding three shape families into one table would obscure why each row exists. 5 skips + 2 live guards can't share a table anyway (`it.skip.each` and `it.each` are separate calls). Tellingly, 7.7 itself declined to fold `bold` into its table because it carried extra assertions. Helper called 7×, varying literal stays at the call site, 136→129. **Mutation-checked on a scratch copy:** all 5 unskipped still fail for their *recorded* reasons, then restored. Suite byte-identical (32 files, 80 passed | 6 skipped), tsc clean — both re-run directly.

The holes exist in the **candidate greens**, not in current code. So these are characterization guards constraining `green-frontend-url-shapes`, not descriptions of a present defect. **Predicted: all four PASS, each dying only under its named mutant. Actual: all four passed. Comparison: all cells YES.** New file `ManualEditor.link.urlShapes.guards.test.tsx` (106 lines after test-review); helpers promoted to a new `ManualEditor.link.testSupport.tsx` (52) which itself imports the generic `ManualEditor.testSupport.tsx` — a correct split, not a duplicate: link-popover flow on top of generic render/select, and merging would push link concerns into a file ~20 ManualEditor tests use, only 3 of which touch links. `ManualEditor.link.urlShapes.test.tsx` 129→82.

  **Every guard mutation-tested against the specific candidate green it targets, by execution — the reflex that has repeatedly caught this scenario's reasoning:**
  | Guard | Verdict | Killed by |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1 — `youtube.com/@vsauce` → `http://` | kill guard | both email-branch candidates |
  | 2 — `https://youtube.com/@vsauce` passes through | **coverage only** | no candidate kills it alone |
  | 3 — `кремль.рф` → `http://кремль.рф` | kill guard | the ASCII host-regex green |
  | 4 — `ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Война_и_мир` | kill guard, **wrong stated reason** | a `\w`-path-class green |

  **My framing for this step was wrong twice, both caught by execution, and the pattern is now the story of this scenario.** (a) I briefed that guard 2 kills the `!startsWith('mailto:')` green — the red-agent found guard 1 kills it too, and kept guard 2 on the narrower rationale that it uniquely pins a *scheme'd* `@`-URL surviving. (b) test-review then found even that narrower claim doesn't hold: it ran two candidates, both died on guards 1 *and* 2 together, and the reason is structural — every `@`-based predicate loose enough to misfire on the scheme'd form is at least as loose on the bare form, because each schemeless discriminator exempts guard 2's input and not guard 1's. Guard 2 fires alone only for a predicate keying on something *only the scheme adds*, which nobody writes. Worse, its comment claimed "the row above **cannot** catch it" — a reasoned claim contradicted by execution, left in prose *after* the red-agent had already corrected the same claim once. Kept, relabelled coverage-only (the fixtures pin a scheme surviving and an `@` surviving, never both together); not deleted, because that is a scope judgement rather than a strict-assertion fix.

  **Guard 4 exposed the redundancy one row from where the file thought it was.** Its comment named the all-ASCII gate — but that candidate leaves `кремль.рф` bare too, so guard 3 already kills it, and the comment concedes this ("leaves BOTH") without noticing it destroys its own rationale. The guard still earns its keep against a green nobody had named: a unicode-aware host check with a `\w`-based path class. **`\w` stays ASCII-only even under `/u`** — the classic JS trap. Executed, that green passes all 5 reds, all 7 fixtures and guard 3, and fails only guard 4. Rationale replaced with the verbatim regex.

  **One real strictness gap, fixed:** `expectSoleLink` pinned the anchor but never the surrounding document, so a green that linked correctly while dropping the unselected `" world"` would pass all 4 guards and all 7 fixtures — the anchor is right, nothing notices. `ManualEditor.link.test.tsx` already pinned `contentArea.textContent`; the helper now does too. `queryByRole('alert')` is in the shared helper, so all 4 guards carry it — the right shape here, since every candidate rendered *silently* wrong (failed on href, never on the alert).

  Full unfiltered suite: **33 files, 84 passed | 6 skipped (90)** — +4 passed, **no new skips** (nothing was red). `tsc -b` clean.

  **Process note worth keeping:** `git checkout --` on the urlShapes file was blocked, correctly — it had uncommitted work and the restore would have taken the 129→82 refactor with it. A scratchpad backup was used instead. That reflex is live in this tree.
- [x] green-frontend-url-shapes — fix `normalizeHref` in `LinkPopover.tsx`. **Two independent fixes, not one** (mutation-confirmed by `/refactor`): `HAS_SCHEME` is the sole cause for the host-port rows, but the email rows fail *past* it — `user@example.com` has no colon at all, so tightening the regex alone leaves `http://user@example.com` intact.

  **The interrupted candidate's two suspected failures were both real, and both landed exactly where the note predicted — resolved by executing, not by re-reading.** The prior note recorded a committed-but-unverified `normalizeHref` (three outcomes, order host-shape → scheme → email → fallback) with `HOST_SHAPE = /^[\p{L}\p{N}-]+(\.[\p{L}\p{N}-]+)*(:\d+)?([\/?#][\w\/%.-]*)?$/u`. **Predicted: guards 1 and 4 fail, guard 3 and the 5 reds pass. Actual: exactly 2 failed / 10 passed** — guard 1 `AssertionError: expected 'youtube.com/@vsauce' to be 'http://youtube.com/@vsauce'`, guard 4 `expected 'ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Война_и_мир' to be 'http://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Война_и_мир'`, both on the href assert inside `expectSoleLink`, both falling through to the bare-return fallback. **Comparison: all cells YES.** The candidate was the mutant guard 4 names verbatim — *"a unicode-aware host check with a `\w`-based path class"* — written after the guard that exists to kill it. The guards did their job; the finding is that the note was not read before the code was.

  **Fix — the path class, per the red-agent's own instruction, not another host special-case:**

  ```
  const HOST_SHAPE = /^[\p{L}\p{N}-]+(\.[\p{L}\p{N}-]+)*(:\d+)?([\/?#]\S*)?$/u
  ```

  `\S*` is the structural form: **a path ends at whitespace and nowhere else**, and the popover trims before calling. Enumerating path characters is what fails — every enumeration is a new guess about which characters exist (`\w` dropped both `@` and all non-ASCII, two of the four guards, from one class). Comment added above the regex recording this so it is not "simplified" back.

  Full unfiltered suite: **33 files, 89 passed | 1 skipped (90)** — delta from the `red-frontend-url-shapes-2` baseline (84 passed | 6 skipped) is exactly +5 passed / −5 skipped, the five reds unskipping, no other file changing state, total unchanged at 90. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

  **Two real defects found while verifying, neither pinned by any test, both belonging to `normalizeHref`'s discriminator — follow-up red/green steps inserted below (7.2's cursor-fix precedent).** Found by probing the function directly against its own pre-candidate behaviour at `d6eb173`, which is the only way either would have surfaced:

  1. **`tel:79001234567` → `http://tel:79001234567`: a live regression this step introduces.** `d6eb173` returned it unchanged. Root cause is the **mirror image of defect 1** and is undecidable as posed: `localhost:3000` and `tel:79001234567` are structurally identical strings — word, colon, digits. Red pinned the first as a *host* (`localhost:3000`, `myserver:8080`); `tel:` must stay a *scheme*. **No regex over the string's shape can separate them** — only knowledge of which schemes exist can, so the fix is a known-scheme check, which is not a host special-case and is not what the red-agent's note forbade. `tel:+79001234567` survives by accident (`+` is not a digit, so `(:\d+)?` misses) — it is the plusless form, i.e. the ordinary one, that breaks. Not fixed here: `tdd-rules.md:24-25` scopes green to minimal code against existing tests and makes tests read-only, so green may neither add the guard nor the test for it.
  2. **The fallback (`LinkPopover.tsx:24`, `return url`) is reachable, unpinned, and now wrong in a new way.** `/docs` → returns `/docs` bare (was `http:///docs` at `d6eb173`); both are wrong, differently. This is **`red-frontend-url-shapes`'s premortem finding, verbatim** ("nothing pins the fallback for input matching none — `/docs` → `http:///docs` or a relative `/docs`, same hole"), now confirmed by execution *and* by coverage. A bare `/docs` is a relative URL against our own origin — precisely the harm `LinkPopover.tsx:7-11` says the prefix exists to prevent.

  **`/test-coverage` (green gate):** `LinkPopover.tsx` — 77.77% stmts, **66.66% branches**, 75% lines; uncovered **24** (the fallback, defect 2 above — 7.9's own executable code, fully unhit, the same class as 7.4's `codeBlockMark.ts:23` and 7.8's `alignCenterMark.ts:17`), **43-45** (the empty→`unsetLink` branch) and **58-59** (`setError(LINK_INVALID_MESSAGE)`). The latter two are pre-existing from `green-frontend`, not this step's — but 58-59 is worth naming: **the rejection signal is untested, and surfacing it is the sole reason the ADR chose the popover over `window.prompt` at all.** The 5 reds each assert `queryByRole('alert')` *absent*; nothing asserts it *present*. Recorded for the popover-contract slices below, not opened as a step here (no defect — the path works, per the ADR's traced `javascript:alert(1)`/`htp://x` rejections).

**Review-pass verdicts on `48e39fe` (green-frontend-url-shapes): agent-review CONCERNS (3 low), premortem CONCERNS (1 credible root), `/refactor` applied one 2-instance inline. Non-gating — the commit stands — but both passes found real gaps, so `red-frontend-url-shapes-3`'s scope above is rewritten rather than merely extended.** Independently re-verified and holding: the suite counts (`33 files, 89 passed | 1 skipped (90)`, and the `+5/-5` delta checked against the 6 `it.skip`s actually present at `fc4bc89`), `tsc` clean, that `862bde5`'s regex is character-for-character the mutant `guards.test.tsx:96` quotes, and that `\S*` and `String.prototype.trim()` share the same whitespace set — so "a path ends at whitespace and nowhere else" has no gap between the regex and the trim.

**The over-match question was answered negative, structurally rather than by sampling** — worth keeping, since it is the reason `\S*` is the right shape and not merely a passing one. `[\w\/%.-] ⊂ \S`, so the change is a pure widening; the newly-admitted set is exactly *paths containing a non-`\w/%.-`, non-whitespace character*. The host can never move (the path group can only begin at `/`, `?` or `#`, all three of which terminate the authority — probed `example.com/\@evil.com`, `example.com#@evil.com`, `x.com/\\evil.com`, all resolve `host=example.com`), and a scheme'd URL can only be caught in the `scheme:digits` shape, which is finding 3 below and predates this diff. No new defect class.

**My own error, recorded because it is this file's recurring one:** `48e39fe`'s commit message says the `tel:` regression is "a regression this commit introduces". **False.** agent-review ran `862bde5`'s regex: it already yielded `http://tel:79001234567`. This commit touches only the path class and `tel:79001234567` has no path, so it cannot have changed it — the regression entered at `862bde5`. The parenthetical ("`d6eb173` passed it through") is true; the claim around it is not. Cause is precise and repeatable: I probed against `d6eb173`'s two-outcome `normalizeHref` and never against `862bde5`'s, then asserted provenance from the gap between them. **Fourth instance of this file's signature error — a claim asserted from the source I happened to read rather than the one that decides it.** True at work-unit granularity (`862bde5` is the same interrupted unit), which is exactly what makes it easy to wave through. Not amended (project rules prefer a new commit); corrected here, where the scenario's history lives.

*agent-review's other two, both accepted and fixed in the refactor commit:* (1) the new comment folded guards 1 and 4 into one stated purpose — guard 4 names the `\w`-path mutant verbatim and exists to kill it, **guard 1 caught it incidentally**, its own kill target being the email-branch candidate. True at file granularity, wrong at row granularity, and row granularity is what this file is fanatical about. Comment corrected. (2) `\S*` is unpinned by any test — folded into `red-frontend-url-shapes-3` above.

*premortem's credible root — the finding that rewrote the follow-up step:* **the `http://` prefix launders input past the only validator.** Detailed as group 1 of `red-frontend-url-shapes-3` above. Two hypotheses it killed by execution and correctly did **not** report: stored XSS via `"` in the path (`\S*` does admit `"`, and `a"onmouseover="alert(1)` is a real attribute-injection shape, but the serializer escapes it to `&quot;` and it round-trips to the identical href — cannot break out of the attribute); and host confusion via `\`/`@`/`//` (authority is always delimited by the first `/?#` and the host class excludes `@`, so no userinfo trick — `new URL()` confirms `host="example.com"` throughout).

**Tooling note worth keeping (`/refactor`):** a probe left in `frontend/src/.../__tests__/` by a concurrently-running review agent made the suite **flaky by construction** — it asserted nothing and appended to a shared output file, so the same tree returned `34 files / 94 passed` and then `34 files / 5 failed | 89 passed` depending on ordering. `/refactor` was one literal instruction ("if a refactoring changes suite counts, revert it") away from reverting a provably inert two-line inline and blaming itself. It also misattributed the probe to the green phase's own verification; the probes were the premortem agent's live scratch and it removed them itself on finishing. Two durable lessons: **`git add -A` is unsafe in this tree while review agents are live** (neither probe path was gitignored — `48e39fe` was verified clean afterwards, 2 files, but by luck of timing), and a suite count taken while an agent is running is not a measurement.

  **pin `normalizeHref`'s *output*, not just its branches.** Scope widened from the original "pin the fallback" by the review passes on `48e39fe`; that framing missed half the defects, for a reason worth stating up front: **under the old `\w` path class the bad inputs below fell to the *fallback*; `\S*` moved them into the `HOST_SHAPE` branch, where they report success.** A red aimed at the fallback would have passed while they shipped. Four groups, one root (the discriminator), one file:

  1. **The `HOST_SHAPE` branch is unrejectable by construction, and nothing pins that it produces a usable href.** Everything it matches is prefixed `http://`, so the scheme is `http` by construction, and `isAllowedUri` — the only downstream validator — vets the **scheme only**. Cheapest fixture: `example.com:99999999999` (port > 65535) → today yields `href="http://example.com:99999999999"` with **no alert**, which `new URL()` itself refuses (`THROWS: Invalid URL`) and `ManualEditor.tsx:56` persists forever. Independently verified by probe, twice. Pin: **a `HOST_SHAPE`-matched input must be rejected when its normalized href does not parse.** This is distinct from the known "rejection signal is untested" gap — there the path exists and is unasserted; here rejection is *unreachable* for the whole branch. (The port half comes from the pre-existing `(:\d+)?`, not the `\S*` diff — but `\S*` is what made the unrejectable set large enough to matter.)
  2. **Invisible and control characters ride through into a confident, dead link.** `\S*` admits U+00AD (Word's soft hyphen — the realistic vector: pasting a wiki URL out of Word), U+200B, U+202E and C0 controls. All verified to yield `http://…`, `alert: NONE`, and `new URL()` parsing **OK** with `host="example.com"` — well-formed-looking and dead on click. Nothing pins this in either direction. Decide the disposition (strip, reject, or accept) rather than leaving it to the regex's silence.
  3. **`tel:79001234567` must pass through unchanged**, while `localhost:3000`/`myserver:8080` keep normalizing to `http://`. The pair is the whole point: `localhost:3000` and `tel:79001234567` are structurally identical — word, colon, digits — so **no regex over the string's shape separates them**; only a known-scheme set can, which is not the host special-case the red-agent's note forbade. A fixture for only one is defeated by the shortcut the other exists to kill (the `red-frontend-url-shapes` ladder, one rung down). The family is wider than `tel`: agent-review probed `file:1`, `ftp:21` and **`javascript:1`** all normalizing to `http://…` today. Include a non-`tel` scheme so green cannot hardcode the name, and keep `javascript:` **rejected** — the prefix currently neuters it by accident, and the fix must not turn it into a live `javascript:` href.
  4. **The fallback must be pinned**: assert what an input matching no branch does (`/docs`, `#anchor`, `?q=1`) — today it returns bare, a relative URL against our own origin, exactly the harm `LinkPopover.tsx:7-11` says the prefix exists to prevent. `LinkPopover.tsx:24` is fully uncovered.

  **Also fold in (agent-review, low): `\S*` itself is unpinned.** An enumeration like `[\p{L}\p{N}\/%.@#?&=_~+:-]*` passes all 89 tests, so the "structural, not enumerated" invariant is held by a code comment alone — a future `/refactor` may re-enumerate, stay green, and re-open the hole. A fixture with a path character outside any plausible enumeration (an emoji, or a rare-script path) would make `\S*` test-forced rather than comment-forced.

  Mutation-check every fixture against the candidate green it targets, per this scenario's standing practice.
  </details>

    reposition the caret) → handler returns early → **popover stuck open AND typed URL silently dropped**. This
    contradicts the ADR's own click-outside row ("Close AND apply the current field value to the captured range")
    — a click into the editor IS outside the popover. The captured-range mechanism exists precisely so
    click-outside can apply after the caret moves; excluding editor DOM defeats it for the case it was built for.
    **Design tension to resolve deliberately in red-2:** should a click into the editor (a) apply+close per the
    ADR, or (b) stay open (current behavior)? (b) diverges from the ADR as written. Lines 99-105 are entirely
    unpinned — no test drives a mousedown inside `editor.view.dom` or `.me-toolbar`.

    `result.content !== sent && editor.getHTML() === sent` — MORE likely to hold because the open popover blurs

The ADR chose the popover partly for jsdom reachability; that argument covers the rejection signal only. **Owe-list (align-design premortem on `2929d74`):** opening the popover with the cursor OUTSIDE a link must render the active-highlight background on `toolbar-link` via `.me-toolbar-btn[aria-expanded='true']` — jsdom applies no CSS so this rule is entirely unguarded; a real-browser computed-style assertion is the only thing that can pin it.
- [S] demo — same reason, no live backend to drive a visible Selenium run against

## Bug: line break in Ручной режим (sprint criterion, 2026-07-20)

Text prints on a single line only — Enter has nowhere to go in the `inline*` document.
Direction fixed by ADR `decisions/line-break-in-inline-doc-decision.md` (**A′**: enable
`hardBreak` + `appendTransaction` stripping the trailing hardBreak node). RED already
pinned and committed (`d12f4a2`), currently `describe.skip` in `ManualEditor.lineBreak.test.tsx`
(`d17eac6`) pending this decision.

- [x] red-frontend-line-break — RED pinned in `ManualEditor.lineBreak.test.tsx`: captures the
  save payload via the `saveDocument` mock (`calls[0][1]` = the `content` arg = `getHTML()`,
  `useDocumentSave.ts:84`) and asserts `line one<br>line two` round-trips exactly — one `<br>`,
  no trailing `<br>`. Verified RED: with `hardBreak: false` the `<br>` is dropped by schema
  reconciliation, payload collapses to `line one line two` (break → space), round-trip assert
  fails. test-review: PASS (strict `toBe`, argument-not-mock-return confirmed).
- [x] green-frontend-line-break — done, **106/106** `src/features/generation` green, tsc clean,
  `ManualEditor.lineBreak.test.tsx` un-skipped and passing. **Mechanism deviated from the ADR's
  first draft** (ADR updated to match): the planned `appendTransaction` delete-strip is unviable —
  its delete re-renders the trailing-break helper which `domObserver.flush()` reparses into a new
  hardBreak in the same dispatch → OOM strip↔reparse loop. Fix removes both trailing-`<br>` sources
  at origin instead: `hardBreakNode.ts` (required `marker` attr disqualifies hardBreak from the
  `inline*` schema `defaultType` ghost-filler + parse-rule ignores `br.ProseMirror-trailingBreak`
  cursor helper) and `hardBreakKeymap.ts` (plain Enter + Shift-Enter → insert hardBreak). Files 42 +
  26 lines. `StarterKit hardBreak:false` kept only to avoid a schema-name collision with the custom
  node. See ADR Implementation note.
- [x] test-coverage — done, **6 tests** added in `ManualEditor.lineBreak.coverage.test.tsx` (150
  lines), suite **112/112** green, tsc clean, no production change, no defect surfaced. Keymap Enter
  path drove via `fireEvent.keyDown` in jsdom (real coverage, not deferred). Covered: real
  Enter/Shift-Enter (count = 1, guards double-insert), stray-trailing prevented, intentional trailing
  KEPT, interior not over-stripped, load/paste `<br>` round-trip (no marker parse error). The cases:
    1. *Real Enter keystroke, not just programmatic `<br>`.* Fire the actual Enter key event on
       the editor and assert `getHTML()` gains one `<br>` at the caret (validates the green keymap;
       RED only exercised the `innerHTML`+`fireEvent.input` domObserver path).
    2. *Trailing-strip actually fires.* Content ending in a break `line one<br>` → getHTML
       `line one` (zero `<br>`); multiple trailing `a<br><br>` → `a`; legacy-reopen self-heal (init a
       saved doc ending in `<br>`, first transaction strips it) — ADR edge table. Plus empty-doc no-op.
    3. *No over-strip of an interior break + live-DOM render.* Mixed `line one<br>line two<br>` →
       getHTML exactly `line one<br>line two` (interior survives, only trailing removed); assert the
       live content area renders two visual lines (guard getHTML/DOM divergence).
    4. *Keymap path has ZERO coverage (agent-review CONCERNS, commit `5a1c1b2`).* The RED/green only
       exercised the DOM-parse path (`innerHTML`+`fireEvent.input`); `hardBreakKeymap.ts` and its
       `insertContent({type:'hardBreak', attrs:{marker:'br'}})` are never executed by the suite. If
       the `marker` attr is renamed/made optional or the keymap drifts to `setHardBreak()` (throws on
       the required attr), Enter breaks silently and the suite stays green. Fire an Enter keydown in
       the editor, assert exactly ONE `<br>` lands in the save payload (count assertion, not "a break
       appears" — guards premortem #2 double-insert).
    5. *Pin the decided trailing-break behavior (premortem #1; ADR edge table already reconciled
       2026-07-20).* Decision: STRAY breaks are prevented, an INTENTIONAL trailing break is KEPT.
       Pin both sides: (a) typed non-empty content has no stray trailing `<br>` in `getHTML()` (stray
       side — already covered by the RED for interior content, extend to the ghost-filler/helper
       path); (b) a keymap Enter at end-of-content → the `<br>` IS present in the save payload
       (intentional side); (c) `setContent('foo<br>')`→`getHTML()` round-trip preserves the break with
       no parse error (load/paste path, required-`marker` supplied by the attr's parseHTML). Live-
       browser proof of no-double-insert + real trailing behavior is owed by the deferred
       green-selenium step below.
- [S] refactor — original intent (extract `stripTrailingHardBreak`) moot (green abandoned the strip
  extension). The fix ships as two dedicated per-extension files (`hardBreakNode.ts` 42,
  `hardBreakKeymap.ts` 26 — both ≤200, matching `horizontalRuleNode.ts` layout). refactor-agent
  already scanned these exact files in the green batch (`5a1c1b2`): verdict NO ACTION (naming clear;
  marker-attr overlap with `horizontalRuleNode.ts` is the intentional one-customization-per-file
  convention, extraction would add indirection). No cross-file smell — nothing to refactor.
- [x] green-selenium — **GREEN live 2026-07-22 — but only the RENDER half of the owe-list; see the
  correction at the end of this entry, the "all three closed" claim first written here was wrong.** New
  `test_manual_editor_line_break_acceptance.py` (2 tests) + `manual_editor_line_break_statements.py`,
  driven against the full stack in a real Chrome with a real backend-issued session.
  (i) single Enter → **exactly one** `<br>` (`foo<br>bar`), asserted as a COUNT so a double-insert
  cannot pass; (ii) **the premortem's CREDIBLE multi-trailing suspicion was a jsdom artifact, not a
  product defect** — a real browser keeps both breaks (`foo<br><br>baz`) where jsdom collapsed them
  to zero; behavior now pinned; (iii) breaks render as real visual lines, asserted via distinct
  `getClientRects()` baselines (2 and 3), so a `<br>` that stops breaking the line visually fails
  even though the HTML still matches.
  **Prediction MISSED, and the miss was informative:** predicted both tests PASS first run
  (characterization of already-shipped behavior, pre-probed live). Actual: both FAILED —
  `foobar<br>` instead of `foo<br>bar`. Cause was the TEST, not the product: `type_text_in_editor`
  focuses by clicking, and a click re-places the caret where the pointer landed, so the
  continuation typed *before* the break. Added `continue_typing_in_editor` (types at the current
  caret, no click); any future multi-keystroke Selenium sequence must click once to focus and then
  keep typing. **Mutation-check (teeth):** removed `HardBreakKeymap` from `ManualEditor.tsx` →
  both tests FAIL; restored (`git checkout`, diff clean) → both PASS. Suite:
  **6 passed, 1 skipped**.
  **CORRECTION (agent-review CONCERNS ×3 + premortem CONCERNS, 3 CREDIBLE — both passes flagged the
  same overstatement independently):** this step did NOT close the whole owe-list. Every assertion
  reads the *view* DOM (`editor-content-area` innerHTML); the save path serializes
  `editor.getHTML()` from the ProseMirror **document model**, a different producer. Owe-list item (i)
  was phrased "exactly one `<br>` lands in the **save payload**" — what shipped proves it lands in
  the **rendered content area**. Still genuinely owed, and both are data-loss-shaped rather than
  cosmetic:
    (a) *save payload* — assert the outbound PUT body (or `editor.getHTML()`) carries exactly one
        `<br>` after one Enter. A break that exists as a view artifact but not as a `hardBreak` node
        passes all three current assertions and saves nothing;
    (b) *reopen round trip* — type with a break, save, reload the document, assert the break
        survived. Nothing in this commit crosses the backend, so a sanitizer or parse rule that
        drops `<br>` is invisible. This is precisely the shape carryover's "a green suite is not
        evidence" keeps producing.
  Two smaller test-side findings, non-data-loss: `continue_typing_in_editor` is silently
  order-dependent (WebDriver focuses an unfocused element and a contenteditable focus puts the caret
  at the START, so a first caller without a prior click gets text *prepended*, with no error — the
  docstring states the contract, nothing enforces it); and `assert_break_count_is` counts the literal
  substring `"<br>"` while the strip matches one exact literal, so any `<br>` that gains an attribute
  is neither stripped nor counted — `querySelectorAll('br:not(.ProseMirror-trailingBreak)').length`
  is the non-brittle form. Also untested: a TRAILING Enter with nothing typed after it (both tests
  type after every Enter, so the strip's interaction with a genuine trailing break is unexercised).
  Historical deferral follows. ~~[S] deferred: same "live app to drive Selenium" gap as the rest of
  this file (see RECONCILE_05); real-browser Enter keystroke bypasses the domObserver path
  that the unit test exercises, so a live run is the only proof the in-browser Enter produces
  exactly one `<br>`. Owe it when the stack runs. **Owe-list (review passes on `cffedc7`):**
  (i) single Enter → exactly one `<br>` (no double-insert from ProseMirror's own contenteditable
  handler, which jsdom cannot exercise); (ii) **≥2 consecutive Enters at end-of-content** —
  premortem CREDIBLE: in jsdom two Enters collapse to zero breaks (`foo`+2×Enter → `foo`). Likely a
  jsdom fake-caret artifact (2nd keyDown doesn't reposition the caret), but could be a real
  multi-trailing bug — a live browser is the only place to tell; pin the intended behavior there;
  (iii) the saved `<br>` actually renders as a visual line break.~~

**Cleanup follow-up (agent-review on `cffedc7`, non-gating):** `ManualEditor.lineBreak.coverage.test.tsx:80`
`expect(sent).not.toContain('ProseMirror-trailingBreak')` is a dead assertion — `getHTML()` serializes
from the model and never emits that view-only cursor-helper class, so it is always-true. The real stray
guard is the trailing-`<br>` regex on the next line. Delete line 80 or repoint it at the live view DOM
on the next touch of this file.

What is left is two ordered tracks. **Track A is jsdom-only (no infra) — do it first.** Track B needs
the live stack; **the stack is already up on this host** (`docker ps`: `infra-backend-1` :8100,
`infra-postgres-1`, `infra-redis-1`, vite dev :5173), and every acceptance run MUST export

container (`random_coffee_app`) and 404s on `/api/v1/auth/register`. Canonical frontend typecheck is
`tsc -b --noEmit` (NOT plain `tsc --noEmit`). Work top-to-bottom; each is a full red→green→refactor unit.

  mount), so `GET /api/v1/documents` (list) identifies it without the app exposing an id to the DOM; the
  saved-content read uses the same access token the browser stored in `sessionStorage`
  (`textery.auth.accessToken`, `Authorization: Bearer`). Teeth: the assertion pins an EXACT `<br>` count of 1
  (fails for 0 = break dropped by a sanitizer/parse rule, and for 2 = double-insert) AND — after a premortem
  CONCERNS on `4498f0b` (the count + independent `foo`/`bar` membership was position-blind, would false-pass

  in follow-up commit):** (1) `aria_role=='textbox'` was vacuous — a contenteditable root computes `textbox`

- [x] green-selenium-4.2-save-queue — **GREEN live 2026-07-23 (`1 passed in 11.46s`, headless Chrome, backend
  :8100).** Scenario 4.2's out-of-order save queue exercised through REAL clicks with a real PUT in flight.
  New `test_manual_editor_save_queue_acceptance.py` (34 lines) + `manual_editor_save_queue_statements.py`
  (~70 lines, extends `ManualEditorSavePayloadStatements` to reuse the API read-back + save locators). Flow:
  type `v1`, throttle the network via CDP `Network.emulateNetworkConditions` (latency 2500ms) so the first
  PUT stays open, click Save, WAIT for the `save-spinner` (proof the first save is genuinely in flight), then
  type `v2` (→ `v1v2`) and click Save AGAIN while the first is still open — the design queues this (sets
  `saveAgainRequested`, consumed in the first save's `.then`) rather than firing a concurrent request. Clear
  the throttle, wait for `Сохранено`, then read the document BACK through `GET /api/v1/documents/{id}` and
  assert the content is exactly `v1v2`. Teeth: the first PUT's body was captured at its click = `v1`, so a
  final `v1v2` REQUIRES the queued second save to have fired with the latest content — a broken/dropped queue
  would leave the backend at the stale `v1` and fail. The spinner gate guarantees the second click lands
  in-flight, not after settle. Needs `BACKEND_PORT=8100`. **Review passes on `a35f446` (both CONCERNS, FIXED
  in follow-up):** both passes found the second CLICK is NOT the queue trigger — `onUpdate -> noteEdit`
  (`ManualEditor.tsx:99`) arms `saveAgainRequested` on the `v2` KEYSTROKE while `isSaving`, so the click is a
  realistic user action but not load-bearing; the original narrative overclaimed it, and content-only teeth
  couldn't distinguish a genuine queue from two sequential saves or concurrent PUTs. Corrected the narrative
  to credit the mid-flight edit, and added `assert_exactly_two_document_puts` — counts PUT `/documents/{id}`
  requests over the run via the driver's already-enabled `goog:loggingPrefs performance` log and asserts
  exactly 2 (initial + one queued re-save): ONE would mean a dropped queue, THREE+ a regression to concurrent
  PUTs (the version-race the strictly-sequential queue prevents). Re-verified GREEN live (`1 passed`).
- [x] green-selenium-7.9-popover-clip — **GREEN live 2026-07-23 (`2 passed in 16.18s`, headless Chrome, backend
  :8100). NO DEFECT — the clip does NOT occur; already mitigated.** New
  `test_manual_editor_popover_clip_acceptance.py` (34 lines, two viewports) +
  `manual_editor_popover_clip_statements.py` (~65 lines, extends `ManualEditorStatements`). Opens the link
  popover and measures `getBoundingClientRect` for the popover, the `.me-editor-shell`, and the viewport via
  one `execute_script`, then asserts the popover's rect fits inside the shell's rect (the `overflow:hidden`
  clip box, 1px tolerance) AND inside the viewport — checked at the DEFAULT desktop width AND at a narrow
  390×740 phone viewport (where the toolbar wraps and the button can sit near the right edge, the worst case).
  Both pass: the feared z-index-can't-escape-overflow:hidden clip is already prevented by the popover CSS from
  prior scenario-7.9 work — `width: min(260px, calc(100vw - 32px))` + `max-width: calc(100vw - 32px)` clamp the
  width, and `@media (max-width:768px)` re-anchors `right:0` so a right-edge button opens leftward. So this is
  a live characterization confirming no regression, not a fix. Teeth: the assertion is a strict rect-containment
  check (fails the moment any edge crosses the shell/viewport bound), exercised at the two widths where a clip
  would actually manifest. **Review passes on `275f090`: agent-review PASS** (verified `getBoundingClientRect`
  reports the element's own box, NOT truncated by the ancestor's `overflow:hidden` — so the test genuinely
  *can* detect a clip, not measure a pre-clipped rect). **premortem CONCERNS (the narrow test might not
  reproduce the worst case → "no clip" unproven) — DISPROVEN by mutation-check:** neutralizing the
  `@media (max-width:768px){ .me-link-popover{ right:0 } }` re-anchor made the narrow test FAIL (`popover
  right=668 > shell right=473, clipped RIGHT`), so the test DOES reproduce the right-edge worst case and the
  CSS mitigation is load-bearing — real teeth, not a vacuous pass. (Chrome's minimum window width floored the
  effective narrow viewport at ~504px, still `<768px` so the `@media` regime applies.) CSS restored clean;
  both tests GREEN after restore.
- [x] green-selenium-beforeunload-prompt — **GREEN live 2026-07-23 (`1 passed in 10.95s`, headless Chrome,
  backend :8100). Partial by design — the honestly-assertable half is proven; two probes established the rest
  is not observable in headless.** New `test_manual_editor_beforeunload_acceptance.py` (30 lines) +
  `manual_editor_beforeunload_statements.py` (~55 lines, extends `ManualEditorSavePayloadStatements`).
  **PROVEN live:** the guard's `useEffect` binds a `beforeunload` listener on the REAL window while the doc is
  dirty and its cleanup REMOVES it once a save leaves it clean — dispatch a genuine cancelable `beforeunload`
  through Chrome's own event system and read `defaultPrevented`: `true` on the fresh (dirty) doc → save →
  `false` once clean. Both polarities have teeth (unbound effect fails the armed assertion; missing cleanup
  fails the disarmed one). This is more than the jsdom unit test in that it exercises the real `window`
  listener + real effect cleanup, not a simulated event target. **NOT assertable in headless (both probed, not
  assumed):** (1) the VISIBLE native "Leave site?" dialog — headless Chrome auto-handles the beforeunload
  dialog on a real reload, no catchable alert surfaced (probe: `PROBE_RESULT=NO_DIALOG`); (2) the
  BeforeUnloadEvent STRING `returnValue` — a synthetic `new Event('beforeunload')` exposes only the legacy
  boolean `returnValue` getter (`!defaultPrevented`) in EVERY engine: real Chrome reported
  `returnValue: false` exactly as jsdom does, so the string form the guard sets (`returnValue = ''`) is
  unobservable without a real user-driven navigation the driver cannot surface. Those two stay runtime
  behavior, documented in the statements module, NOT falsely claimed as covered. Same live-efficacy limit
  applies to `auth/utils/useUnsavedGuard.ts` (identical pattern). **Review passes on `96e51b4`: agent-review
  PASS** (both polarities teethed; `SAVED_STATUS` ⟺ `!hasUnsavedChanges` so disarmed can't false-pass; headless
  limitation accurate). **premortem CONCERNS (credible) — FIXED in follow-up:** it distinguished the
  *unobservable* part (dispatching a synthetic event → legacy boolean; the visible dialog) from the
  *observable* part I had over-generalized as unobservable — the `event.returnValue = ''` ASSIGNMENT is a plain
  field write, untested at ANY layer (jsdom + this both declined it), yet a refactor dropping it would silently
  stop the native prompt on legacy Chrome/Edge + older Safari/Firefox. Closed one layer down: added a unit test
  to `ManualEditor.beforeUnloadGuard.test.tsx` that calls the CAPTURED guard handler DIRECTLY with a mock event
  (`{preventDefault, returnValue: undefined}`) and asserts `returnValue === ''` + `preventDefault` called — the
  one place that line has teeth (mock starts `undefined`, so dropping the assignment fails). generation suite
  green, tsc `-b --noEmit` clean. **Owed (not this story's file):** `auth/utils/useUnsavedGuard.ts` has the
  identical untested `preventDefault`-only pattern (no `returnValue`) — a separate auth-side follow-up.

**🎉 ALL Track A + Track B work complete — the "Remaining work (ready for /loop)" section is fully done.**
What remains for Story 5 frontend is only the **Owed follow-ups** below (product decisions / larger scope),
which are intentionally not checkboxes.

### Owed follow-ups (not yet checkboxes — need a product decision)

- **guard-unsaved-work spurious prompt** (both review passes on `24269ba`, CONCERNS): `hasUnsavedChanges`
  initialises `true` (ManualEditor.tsx:41) and only clears on a resolved save, so the `beforeunload` guard
  is ARMED the instant any editor mounts — a user who opens a manual doc, reads it, and leaves WITHOUT
  typing still gets the "leave? unsaved changes" prompt. The current guard tests codify this dirty-on-mount
  as intended. Fixing it (arm only after the first real edit) is a semantics change to the same flag that
  drives the "Черновик, ещё не сохранён" draft status (scenarios 5.1/5.2), so it needs a product call, not a
  drive-by edit. Decide: (a) seed dirty from actual edit activity and split "has content" from "has unsaved
  user edits", or (b) accept the prompt on any open and pin that decision. Also owed: the full
  `localStorage`/sessionStorage draft so "leave" doesn't still lose everything (the prompt only warns).
- **useBeforeUnloadGuard extraction** (refactor pass on `24269ba`, NO ACTION-owed): ManualEditor's guard
  effect duplicates the addEventListener/preventDefault/removeEventListener shape in
  `auth/utils/useUnsavedGuard.ts`. Extracting a shared generic `useBeforeUnloadGuard(active)` is a real
  de-dup but should ride WITH a rework of the auth hook (its ref-based design + confirmLeave/markDirty API),
  not force a risky one-off conversion of auth to reactive state now.

## Editor Extension — "full manual mode" (points 1–8)

New scope arrived 2026-07-26 via `editor-extension.md` + `tests/07_Editor_Extension_Tests.md`
+ `tests/08_Editor_Extension_Hazard_Tests.md`. Frontend-owned (session C) except the `title`
backend column (session A, tracked in `progress-backend.md`). **Block schema is the root
blocker — everything below stands on it; sequence it first.** Each scenario is a full
frontend TDD unit (`red-frontend` → `/test-review` → commit → `/refactor` → commit →
`green-frontend` → commit → `/refactor` → commit → `green-selenium` for browser-only
surfaces → `demo`). Steps not applicable to a given scenario are marked `[S]` with a reason.
Backend-only hazard guards (server re-sanitize, mass-assignment, title-length unit, rolling
deploy) live in `progress-backend.md`, referenced here as `[S]`.

### Scenario E1.1: Multi-paragraph block content round-trips (block schema) — ROOT BLOCKER
- [x] red-frontend — block schema: paragraphs + H1/H2/H3 as block nodes; save→reload round-trips as semantic HTML
- [x] design — ADR `decisions/block-schema-migration-decision.md`: full StarterKit block model; retire custom marks + bespoke line-break machinery; align → textAlign attr; strip trailing empty `<p>`; the ~40 inline-schema sibling assertions are rewritten to block expectations in the green unit, **authorized by the ADR** (green's tests-read-only rule is lifted for this scenario)
- [x] red-rework + green-frontend (bundled per ADR) — migrate `Document` to `block+`, enable StarterKit paragraph/heading(1-3)/lists/blockquote/codeBlock/hr/hardBreak nodes, drop `Heading3Mark`/`BlockquoteMark`/`CodeBlockMark`/`HorizontalRuleNode`/`HardBreakNode`/`HardBreakKeymap`/`InlinePlaceholder`, move toolbar `isActive`/commands to node names, add `Placeholder` extension keeping the pinned surfaces; rewrite every inline-schema sibling assertion (`reopen`, `*.parseHTML`, `blockquote`, `dirty`, `textAlign`, `inlineCode`, `underline`, `horizontalRule`, `heading3.parseHTML`) to block-wrapped expectations; add a mixed inline-marks-inside-blocks round-trip test (premortem #2); strip trailing empty `<p>` on serialize
- [x] green-selenium — live round-trip through `PUT`/`GET /documents/{id}` (test_manual_editor_block_schema_acceptance.py; builds H1/H2/H3+paragraph via StarterKit input rules, reads back via backend GET, exact semantic-HTML assertion)
- [S] demo — skipped in unattended loop (no viewer to watch the visible browser); E1.1 behavior proven by the passing headless green-selenium round-trip. Run `/demo TestManualEditorBlockSchemaAcceptance` to watch it live.

Glyph-vs-icon (`•`/`1.` text vs lucide `list`/`list-ordered`) is a global pre-existing toolbar decision applied to every button, out of this scenario's scope. /design-review PASS (no hardcoded placeholder data; testIds + shared class confirmed). /test-coverage: list-action branches 100% covered.

### Scenario E3.1: Edits autosave without an explicit click (debounced)
- [x] red-frontend — debounced autosave over existing `PUT /documents/{id}`; saved indicator shown. `ManualEditor.autosave.test.tsx` (`it.skip`): edits without a click → after the debounce fires exactly one `saveDocument('doc-1', '<p>hello world</p>', 7)` and shows "Сохранено"; no save at 999ms, one at 1000ms (assumed `AUTOSAVE_DEBOUNCE_MS=1000`). Fails today: saveDocument 0 calls (no debounce exists). GREEN owes the debounced autosave in `useDocumentSave.ts` + `ManualEditor.tsx` onUpdate wiring.

Un-skipped `ManualEditor.autosave.test.tsx`; added multi-edit-collapse + unmount-cancel guards (both green). Frontend 516 passed, 0 skipped. ManualEditor.tsx 182 lines, useAutosave.ts 40.

Guard pins the combination for the AUTOSAVE path: saveDocument rejects after the debounce → `me-save-error`/SAVE_ERROR_MESSAGE shown AND `editor-content-area` still exactly `<p>hello world</p>`. Both assertions mutation-checked to bite (red-agent + test-review).

### Scenario E3.3 / H9.2: Out-of-order autosave responses reflect the latest edit and content
- [x] red-frontend — two saves in flight resolving out of order: status + content reflect latest (B), B not lost. LIVE **characterization guard** (`ManualEditor.autosaveOutOfOrder.test.tsx`, 1 passing) — not a red→green cycle. Existence-check: `useDocumentSave.ts` SERIALIZES saves (`isSavingRef`/`saveAgainRequested`) — a save landing mid-flight only flips `saveAgainRequested`; the queued save (B) is fired from A's resolve handler using A's returned version + a fresh editor read, so two `saveDocument` calls are never simultaneously in flight and out-of-order *arrival* cannot occur — latest-wins holds by construction. Guard pins: after A resolves then B resolves, `saveDocument` called with B's content/version (not A's stale one) AND editor content stays B (`<p>second version</p>`) AND status is exactly "Сохранено" (not reverted to dirty). Both content + status assertions mutation-checked to bite (removing the `&& serializeEditorHtml(editor) === sent` anti-clobber guard fails the test); test-review added exact-`textContent` + negative dirty-status assertions.
- [S] green-frontend — no production code needed; latest-wins already guaranteed by the serialized save state machine (see red-frontend note). Zero production files change.
- [S] demo — unattended loop, no viewer; latest-wins behavior proven by the passing characterization guard.

### Scenario E3.4 / H9.5: A stale autosave is rejected (409), never a silent overwrite; version fail-closed
- [x] red-frontend — stale-version autosave → 409 reconciled; absent/unparseable version fails closed. **Two halves:** (1) **409 reconcile / not overwritten → [S]** — already delivered by `saveDocument`'s refetch-retry (documentApi.ts:100-114) and already characterized by the existing guard `documentApi.conflict.test.ts` (retry carries refetched version, gives up after 2nd 409); no new test/production. NB the spec's "not overwritten" is deliberate last-writer-wins on retry (documentApi.ts:94-99 + carryover), a product decision not a gap. (2) **absent/unparseable version fails closed → true failing RED** — `send<DocumentWire>` casts JSON blindly; version never validated, so a create/get response with version missing/`null`/`"abc"`/NaN flows into the autosave PUT as a garbage token (silent-overwrite risk H9.5 forbids). New RED `documentApi.versionFailClosed.test.ts` (76 lines, 4 independent `it.skip` cases — undefined/null/"abc"/NaN) asserts `createDocument` REJECTS with exact message `INVALID_VERSION_MESSAGE` ('Сервер вернул документ без корректной версии.') rather than returning a garbage version. Predicted AssertionError (promise resolves w/ version undefined instead of rejecting) matched actual verbatim. test-review pinned the exact-message contract green must throw.
- [x] green-frontend — version guard on autosave path. Added shared `parseVersion(raw)` at the documentApi.ts parse boundary (throws `INVALID_VERSION_MESSAGE` when `!Number.isFinite`), applied in `createDocument`/`putDocument`/`getDocument` — one guard covers all four feeders incl. the 409→refetch→retry (saveDocument flows through getDocument). `INVALID_VERSION_MESSAGE` now EXPORTED from production and imported by the test (inline literal removed, drift closed). RED-first expansion landed the get/put/refetch cases the review passes demanded: 8 cases failed pre-guard (create/get resolved w/ garbage version; refetch fired a 3rd garbage PUT) → 8 pass post-guard, garbage token never re-PUT (fetchMock called exactly twice). NaN kept in create set (documents contract), dropped from get set (unreachable over real `response.json()`). Full frontend suite 526 passed/0 skipped; `npm run build` exit 0. documentApi.ts 153 lines, test 119.
- [S] follow-up (premortem on ca7245a, non-blocking) — **putDocument-200 garbage-version is an untested branch**: `parseVersion` sits at documentApi.ts:100 (putDocument), but no test drives a *successful* PUT whose 200 body carries a non-finite version — deleting the line-100 guard leaves the suite green. The 409-refetch case trips getDocument's guard, not putDocument's. Add a case: PUT returns 200 with garbage version → `saveDocument` rejects with `INVALID_VERSION_MESSAGE`.
- [S] follow-up (premortem on ca7245a, non-blocking) — **save-path fail-closed shows retry-that-cannot-work banner**: a version-fail-closed on the save path throws a bare `Error(INVALID_VERSION_MESSAGE)`; `describeSaveFailure` (useDocumentSave.ts:34-38) has arms only for `SessionExpiredError`/`VersionConflictError`, so it collapses to generic `SAVE_ERROR_MESSAGE` ("Повторите…") — but retrying re-PUTs the stale version and can only fail again. Init/open path is fine (useDocumentInit surfaces `error.message` verbatim). Fix would need a typed error class (like the other two) rather than message-matching. Decide: typed `InvalidVersionError` + `describeSaveFailure` arm, or accept generic banner.
- [S] follow-up (premortem, non-blocking) — E3.4 half-1 `[S]` closes "409 reconciled" but the reconcile is deliberate **last-writer-wins** (documentApi.ts:94-99): a two-tab concurrent-writer sequence silently discards the other tab's saved paragraph. Single-owner product decision, documented, but the scenario title says "never a silent overwrite" and no test **characterizes** the two-tab outcome. Decide: add a characterization test pinning last-writer-wins as deliberate, or record that half-1's criterion holds only for single-tab reconcile. Needs conflict-resolution UI this story lacks — likely deferred, but must be an explicit decision not a silent gap.
- [x] green-selenium — live 409 reconcile (`test_manual_editor_conflict_reconcile_acceptance.py` + `manual_editor_conflict_reconcile_statements.py`, `1 passed` 18.89s against the live stack backend:8100/frontend:80). Forces a REAL 409: live session → type → autosave settles at version N → out-of-band direct backend PUT at N with different content advances server to N+1 (editor's held N now stale) → type more → autosave PUTs stale N → 409 VERSION_CONFLICT → app's saveDocument refetches N+1 + retries → 200. Asserts `.me-save-status--saved` returns with zero `me-save-error` banners AND strict `content == "<p>original more</p>"` (editor's text wins — last-writer-wins reconcile, out-of-band content overwritten per the documented single-owner trade-off). No production/backend changes. **Strengthened (follow-up, 1 passed 18.14s):** added a `window.fetch`-wrapping PUT-status recorder (`__putStatuses`) installed after the first autosave settles + `assert_stale_put_was_rejected_then_retried` — proves a real `409` was raised THEN a `200` retry followed, so the final-content assertion can no longer pass against a backend that silently accepted the stale overwrite (both land the same bytes). Also added a dirty-status barrier in `assert_autosave_reconciled_without_error` so the saved-wait can't return vacuously on the prior clean save before the reconcile fires.
- [S] demo — unattended loop, no viewer; live 409 reconcile proven by the passing green-selenium test. Run `/demo TestManualEditorConflictReconcileAcceptance` to watch it live.

### Scenario H9.3: Autosave failures handled per kind (transient backoff/cap vs re-auth)
- [x] red-frontend — transient timeout/5xx retries with capped backoff; expired session prompts re-auth. **Also pin two failure-path gaps CONFIRMED by the E3.2 premortem (6a3dbc4):** (a) **queued-edit dropped on failure** — an edit landing during an in-flight save sets `saveAgainRequested=true`; if that save then FAILS, the `.catch` resets `saveAgainRequested=false` (`useDocumentSave.ts:119`) and the queued edit is never re-sent, and autosave only re-arms on a fresh edit → silent data loss if the user stops typing. Bounded retry must re-fire the dropped queued edit (or keep it dirty + retried). (b) **beforeunload-still-armed-after-failed-autosave** — after a failed autosave `hasUnsavedChanges` must stay true so the `beforeunload` guard stays armed (currently correct but UNGUARDED by any test); add a test that dispatches a cancelable `beforeunload` after a rejected autosave and asserts `preventDefault` fired (mirror `beforeUnloadGuard.test.tsx` dirty path). **DONE:** 4 test files under `components/__tests__/` — `ManualEditor.autosaveRetryBackoff.test.tsx` (2 RED it.skip: retry-on-transient re-fires with exact args id/'<p>hello world</p>'/7, and bounded cap at exactly `MAX_AUTOSAVE_ATTEMPTS=4`), `ManualEditor.autosaveFailedQueuedEdit.test.tsx` (1 RED it.skip: failed-save queued edit re-fired, currently 1 call not 2), `ManualEditor.autosaveExpiredSessionNoRetry.test.tsx` (LIVE guard: `SessionExpiredError` prompts re-auth, no retry), `ManualEditor.beforeUnloadAfterFailedAutosave.test.tsx` (LIVE guard: cancelable `beforeunload` after rejected autosave → `preventDefault` fired). Shared magic-const `RETRY_WINDOW_MS` hoisted into `ManualEditor.autosave.testSupport.ts`. Predicted failures matched actual verbatim (call-count 1≠2, cap 1≠4). Green owes bounded-retry+backoff on transient kinds (`RequestTimeoutError`/5xx only) in `useDocumentSave.ts` + re-fire the queued edit instead of dropping `saveAgainRequested` at line 119; may need `httpClient`/`send` to stop flattening transient kinds into plain `Error`. 3 RED skipped, 2 guards pass; `npm run build` exit 0.
- [x] green-frontend — failure taxonomy + bounded retry (must close H9.3(a) queued-edit-dropped above). **Concerns 2 (expired-session-no-retry) & 4 (beforeunload-armed) are already-passing characterization guards → those halves are `[S]` (no production owed); green must not regress them (don't fold `SessionExpiredError` into the retry arm; don't clear dirty / disarm beforeunload on failed autosave).** **DONE (all in `useDocumentSave.ts`, 196 lines):** exported `MAX_AUTOSAVE_ATTEMPTS=4` (test imports it, literal removed — one source of the cap); `isTransientFailure(error)` true only for `RequestTimeoutError` + `HttpError status>=500` (SessionExpired/VersionConflict/4xx/generic stay non-transient → existing arms, both live guards still green); capped exponential `backoffDelay` (1s/2s/4s, ceil 8s) on a `retryTimerRef` cleared on unmount; `.catch` schedules a retry while transient + attempts remain, keeping `isSavingRef` true across the wait so competing saves queue. **Key design that closes BOTH gap (a) AND the premortem backoff-gap concern:** the retry calls `performSave(saveVersion, attempt+1)` which **re-serializes the editor's CURRENT content at fire time**, so the latest content is always what's sent (at the unchanged version — a failed save doesn't bump it) — a queued edit or an edit typed during the backoff wait can never be silently lost. Un-skipped the 2 RetryBackoff + 1 QueuedEdit RED tests; **added a new non-skipped regression test** pinning the backoff-gap (edit during wait → retry re-sends latest content, "Сохранено" only over sent content). H9.3 4 files 6 passed/0 skipped; full frontend suite 532 passed/0 skipped; `npm run build` exit 0.
- [S] follow-up (agent-review on 25c3f12, non-blocking) — **CONCRETE DEFECT: retry firing after `documentId`/`editor` goes null mid-backoff wedges the writer forever.** The scheduled retry calls `performSave(saveVersion, attempt+1)`; its first line `if (!documentId || !editor) return` (useDocumentSave.ts:115-116) executes BEFORE any state reset. On a retry `isSavingRef` is already `true` and is never cleared on this early-return path — so if `editor`/`documentId` nulls between scheduling the timer and its firing (component still mounted, e.g. parent clears `documentId` on a route/state change), `isSaving` sticks `true` forever (toolbar stuck "Сохранение…", no banner) and every future `save()` (line 189) sees `isSavingRef` true → only sets `saveAgainRequested` and returns silently → later edits can never be saved, no error surfaced.   **(A) both message-level assertions re-pointed, neither relaxed nor deleted** — each split into the two halves of the same claim. `saveDocument` 500 (was `rejects.toThrow('Внутренняя ошибка сервера')`) → `toEqual({status: 500, body: {message: 'Внутренняя ошибка сервера'}})` **plus** `describeFailure(rejection, …) === 'Внутренняя ошибка сервера'`: the message now lives in `body`, where a string cannot lose it, and rendering through the helper every consumer calls still yields that exact text. `createGeneration` 500 with an unparseable body (was `rejects.toThrow('HTTP 500')`) → `toEqual({status: 500, body: {}})` **plus** `describeFailure(rejection, 'Не удалось создать запрос') === 'Не удалось создать запрос (HTTP 500)'` — the fallback-text claim is what the test was for, and the shape half pins that the status survives as a **number**, which is what a regression here destroys silently. The `Error`-subclass carrier was not attempted; the RED's `toEqual` forecloses it.

Three new suites drive the real `fetch → httpClient → send → api → hook` chain with **no `vi.mock` of the api module**, asserting user-visible text, each with an origin-message case and a bodiless case pinning that the status still reaches the screen: `ManualEditor.initServerError.test.tsx` (createDocument + getDocument, 3 cases), `useGeneration.serverError.test.ts` (2), `useHistoryList.serverError.test.ts` (2) — that last caller previously had no 5xx case anywhere in the repo. **One behavior change worth knowing:** for a 5xx with no readable body the user now sees the **hook's** fallback, not the api client's, because the api-layer fallback only ever reached them *via* the flatten. Declined for two reasons worth keeping: the required relationship is a **superset, not an equality** (the retry policy can only ever see errors `send` chose to preserve, so correctness needs *preserved ⊇ transient*; today they are equal but that is incidental, and one shared predicate would assert equality permanently, blocking `send` from ever widening — say to a 429 with `Retry-After` — without dragging the policy along); and placement has nowhere clean to go, since `shared/api` cannot import `features/generation`, so it would land in `httpClient.ts`, whose documented invariant is "transport, knows nothing" — "can waiting heal this?" is a policy judgment, not a transport fact. The comment at `send.ts:70-83` already names `autosaveRetryPolicy.ts` and explains *why* they must agree, which a shared function name cannot do.

Under "rethrow 5xx `HttpError` untouched" the rejection is a plain `{status, body}` object, and a plain-object rejection does **not** satisfy `rejects.toThrow` (probed directly in vitest 4.1.10). - [S] red-frontend (premortem CREDIBLE #3 on `9bf064a`, CONFIRMED LIVE by both passes on `82fd240`) — **the retry can wedge the editor permanently instead of settling, and this commit widened the path into it.** `performSave` opens with `if (!documentId || !editor) return` — a bare return calling no `cycle.settle*`, leaving `isSavingRef.current === true`: toolbar stuck on «Сохранение…» forever, `beforeunload` armed, «Сохранить» dead on every click, no banner. `autosaveSaveCycle.ts` names this exact hazard in `settleClean`'s own docblock ("a bare return would strand the badge, beforeunload and the Сохранить button in the dirty state with no path back") — this one return violates it. Previously a revert during the backoff window hit the fire-time gate and exited through `settleClean()`; now that `lastSavedContentRef.current = null` forces that gate false, the same retry falls through into `performSave` and its un-settled early return. The wedge is pre-existing; `9bf064a` routes a class of retries into it that used to bypass it. **Missing guard:** a test firing a scheduled retry with `editor === null` / `documentId === null` asserting the cycle settles — after the retry fires against a null editor, `dispatchBeforeUnload()` is `false` and the badge is not «Сохранение…». **PROMOTED above the two `9bf064a` follow-ups that precede it in file order, because `82fd240` changed its severity:** before the `send` carve-out, `isTransientFailure` was structurally false in production, so no real server error could ever schedule a retry and this wedge was reachable only from a fixture. It is now reachable from a genuine 500 or timeout. Split into red/green because the guard is a test plus a production fix, not a note. **[S] DISPROVED (red-frontend probe, 2026-07-28):** the early return is unreachable from a scheduled retry — the retry callback closes over the `performSave` instance that already passed the `!documentId || !editor` guard, so a later render nulling either prop cannot reach it. Probe (props nulled mid-flight, provably armed: `midCycleIsSaving=true`): retry still writes and settles clean — `saveCalls=2`, `onSaved=1`, final `isSaving=false`, for both the `documentId → null` and `editor → null` rerenders; `save()` from an all-null state schedules nothing (0 calls, 0 pending timers). Secondary: were it ever reached, `serializeEditorHtml(editor)` at the fire-time gate would throw `TypeError` before `performSave`, so the described silent-wedge mechanism is wrong too. The return is dead code — a `/refactor` removal candidate, not a guard gap.
- [S] green-frontend — settle the cycle on the unreachable-target path instead of returning bare: `performSave`'s `if (!documentId || !editor) return` must route through a `cycle.settle*` so `isSavingRef`, the badge, `beforeunload` and the «Сохранить» button all come back. Decide deliberately which settle it is — `settleClean()` claims the write landed and drops the dirty state, `settleFailed()` surfaces a banner for a save the user cannot retry from a torn-down editor; the honest answer is likely `settleFailed` on a null `documentId` (the write is genuinely lost) and a plain settle on unmount (nothing to tell a component that is gone). Keep the whole dirty-guard family and the retry-backoff suite green. **[S] DISPROVED (red-frontend probe, 2026-07-28):** the early return is unreachable from a scheduled retry — the retry callback closes over the `performSave` instance that already passed the `!documentId || !editor` guard, so a later render nulling either prop cannot reach it. Secondary: were it ever reached, `serializeEditorHtml(editor)` at the fire-time gate would throw `TypeError` before `performSave`, so the described silent-wedge mechanism is wrong too.
