- [x] follow-up (agent-review CONCERNS #1 + premortem CREDIBLE #2 on `9bf064a`, the SAME finding from both) — **the only fail-dangerous branch in the change has no executable guard, and the two files a person would be editing when they break it carry no pointer back.** Every `true` from `mayHaveLandedServerSide` costs at most a redundant PUT; `503 → false` is the only answer that can suppress a needed write, and its entire safety argument is the dated prose measurement now living in `autosaveRetryPolicy.ts`'s docblock. Add a rate limiter, readiness probe, or WAF answering 503 downstream of the origin and the branch silently suppresses genuinely-needed writes with nothing red anywhere. Cost/benefit is in the direction with the worse tail: the benefit bought is one avoided redundant PUT (`BackoffRevert`'s `toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2)`), the cost if the premise lapses is exactly the silent divergence under «Сохранено» that H9.4 exists to prevent. A test cannot assert an environment fact, so the guard is a **back-reference, not a test**: a note in `infra/docker/nginx/frontend.conf` beside where a `limit_req`/`error_page` would be added, naming `mayHaveLandedServerSide` as the thing that breaks. Optionally also a backend-side test pinning that no handler returns 503. **DONE (2026-07-28):** `infra/docker/nginx/frontend.conf` now carries the back-reference in the `location /api/` block — names `mayHaveLandedServerSide`, states that 503 makes the retry SKIP the write, enumerates the directives that break the premise (`limit_req`/`limit_conn`, `error_page`, `max_fails`/`proxy_next_upstream`, a WAF), and says the failure is silent («Сохранено» over content the server never got) with no test able to catch it. The optional backend-side test pinning that no handler returns 503 is NOT done: `backend/` is the other layer's file ownership — recorded here as owed, to be picked up by the backend session. **REVIEW FIX (`HEAD+1`, agent-review CONCERNS #1 on `90d4fa1`):** the first comment stated the wrong mechanism — «on that answer the retry SKIPS the write». It does not. `mayHaveLandedServerSide === false` *keeps* `lastSavedContentRef.current` (`useDocumentSave.ts:133`); the write is suppressed only if the user reverts to that content inside the backoff window and the fire-time gate at `:140` matches. A reader following the comment's own instruction to open `autosaveRetryPolicy.ts` would have found it falsifiable on first check and dismissed the whole warning. Corrected in the follow-up commit, together with agent-review #2 (scoped «no test can catch it» to «no frontend test can see a directive added here» — a backend test is still owed) and #3 (widened from this location to «this conf or anything in front of the origin», naming `http`/`server`-level inheritance, a new `upstream` block, and the host/prod-copy proxy).

The mutant passes by luck of declaration order; the shipped shape does not rely on that order, but no test forbids the fragile variant either — which is the second premortem REMOTE below (hook-declaration-order dependency) now upgraded from theory to a demonstrated surviving mutant. **Out of scope, pre-existing:** `npm run lint` errors on `frontend/scripts/check-nginx-503.selftest.mjs:36:10 eslint(no-unused-vars): Function 'backendFixture' is declared but never used` — present at HEAD, last touched in `060631f`, untouched here.

Both committed cases pass for a green keyed on `timerRef !== null`, and the content-diff variant of «key on dirtiness» survives them too: the debounce case never asserts that a timer is PENDING (only that no request fired, the editor holds the text, and `beforeunload` is armed — which the twin itself proves is true with nothing typed). Harm is the one the file's own header claims to fence, reached from the direction the twin was not built for: a false record on every ordinary type-then-undo back-out, which is how the record that matters becomes invisible. RED owes: type, revert to the initial content inside the gap (the `autosaveDirtyGuardRevertedEdit` suite owns that motion), unmount, `expectNoAbandonmentRecorded()` — red on the armed-timer key, green only on a key that consults `lastSavedContentRef`. Consider `getTimerCount()).toBeGreaterThan(0)` as the middle ground the rejected `toBe(1)` had. **WIDENED by both passes on `f24de68` (agent-review CONCERNS #1 + premortem CREDIBLE #1, the same finding again from both) — the green that landed is keyed exactly the way this step predicted, so the step now owns three motions, not one.** `hasPendingEditRef` models «a debounce timer is armed», never «unsent content exists», and the two diverge everywhere the armed write is made moot without the timer firing: (a) **revert-to-saved** in the gap, the original motion; (b) **manual Сохранить inside the gap** — `ManualEditor.tsx`'s Save button calls `save` directly, the write resolves, the badge reads «Сохранено», and the still-armed timer leaves the flag true, so an unmount before t=1000 records an abandonment of nothing (no 5xx, no revert — a keystroke plus a click); (c) **the inert stale timer** — an edit landing mid-flight arms the debounce AND queues a re-save; the queue settles clean and the stale timer is deliberately left armed and inert, so backing out in that residual window records loss on a clean document while `beforeunload` reads `false` at the same instant. Neither twin covers any of the three: the settled-write twin uses `typeAndFireAutosave`, which lets the timer fire and so exercises the one path that DOES clear the flag, and the untouched-document twin never sets the flag at all. **Fix shape both passes converge on:** clear the flag where the WRITE is decided against — `save()`'s two early returns (`!documentId`, `isAlreadySaved`) — not where the timer happens to fire. RED owes at least (a) and (b); (c) is the same key and can ride along.
